# Unfilter digest 03 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

**Read and written 2026-11-17.** Share: voice/reddit-ur03.jsonl (505 quotes), voice/reddit-ur20.jsonl (362), and voice/reddit-ur37.jsonl (333): **1,200 quote records**. Read the niche README, current findings and each ledger’s “What these posts add” section. The JSONL records control every ID and quotation; ledger example IDs were checked against them rather than copied mechanically.

This is an exhaustive digest of a deliberately selected re-read share, not a sample of prevalence. All counts below count quote records, never people. Several IDs can come from one post or one author; a later improvement and an earlier difficulty can be the same account. No network or git was used. Source statements about risks, diagnoses, medication, law, services and policy remain attributed testimony. All 1,200 quote fields are reproduced in full, including sensitive subjects, uncertainty, spelling, profanity and contrary accounts. No new diagnosis, efficacy claim or customer valuation is inferred.

The pattern summaries are interpretation. Each current-conclusion excerpt is checked literally against synthesis/findings.md. Each evidence block is the complete quote field for its named ID. Context/stage/speaker labels are copied from the record and do not establish facts beyond the speaker’s account. Cross-references after each pattern point to evidence also reproduced elsewhere. Money and Requests intentionally repeat the relevant passages so those audits are usable on their own.

## Patterns and their evidence

<a id="pattern-a"></a>

### A. A reassuring result does not end fear when symptoms, bodily changes and previous losses keep changing what reassurance means.

**Belongs in:** §2 (first trimester); §6 (waiting and reassurance); §§16.4 and 17.6 (contrary evidence).

**Relation:** Extends; contradicts a universal reassurance script.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `More information can reassure or renew fear.`

**What should change or remain:** Explain the specific missed-miscarriage mechanism: the person previously felt pregnant, had good numbers or a heartbeat, and learned weeks later the pregnancy had ended. Feeling better, continuing to feel pregnant, a lighter test, blood, no blood, movement and no movement can each become alarming. A calendar or affirmation cannot be assumed reassuring. The therapist’s grounding exercises help one author while “I am pregnant” makes the same author worse. Include the wait for location/viability assessment, questions about repeat scans and the difference between pregnancy symptoms and proof of viability as expressed needs. Fear also occurs without a previous loss; do not give every anxious account a loss history.

**Reading the evidence:** The proposed purchase of a Doppler and repeated tests/scans are attempts to know, while deliberately buying nothing protects against an imagined loss. A BP monitor can prompt useful contact and can also recall earlier pre-eclampsia. The quotes below include both reactions, contradictory interpretations and uncertainty, not clinical conclusions.

**Evidence (229 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0133]** — Author of “Freaking out at loss of symptoms at 10+3 weeks - looking for good outcome stories!!”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I'm alone, my husband is abroad for another week for work and I'm spiralling that I haven't got my usual symptoms. 
> 
> I've had no bleeding, no spotting, minor cramps.
> 
> I've been sat poking my boobs for the past few hours and they don't really hurt..... 
> 
> Has anyone had loss/reduced symptoms around this time and had successful pregnancies? 

**[pbu03-0256]** — Author of “How to know he/she is ok?”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I thought about buying a Doppler but apparently it can be misleading.   
> I started today to measure my belly and write it in a calendar to see if it grows week by week (I already started to see a belly on me). What other ways do you do to reassure yourself that baby is fine? Somehow one ultrasound per month is not enough for me :(

**[pbu03-0438]** — Author of “Really dark thoughts surrounding outcome of pregnancy, advice needed?”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I haven’t bought anything, have no intentions of buying anything, I’ve packed some of a hospital bag but only because I know, either way, I will need that.  
> I feel like if I buy clothes it will just be a) a waste of money and b) buying something that I’ll never use because of the above.

**[pbu20-0031]** — Reduced symptoms before movements create an unsettling gap between scans. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Maybe there is no answer to this question…but HOW am I supposed to assume everything is fine between now and my next scan? are there annnnny indicators that things are progressing normally during this “in between” phase where you feel relatively normal but haven’t reached a point of being able to feel the baby? Is there anything besides the obvious absence of red flag symptoms that I can take solace in? I really want to get over this chronic nervousness. :(

**[pbu20-0355]** — Helpful grounding and unhelpful affirmation occur in same therapy session after missed miscarriage. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> She gave me some grounding exercises for when I start having physical symptoms of anxiety, which have been very helpful. But when we started working on stopping the intrusive thoughts and the spiraling things went downhill . She told me to say "In this moment, I am pregnant."
> 
> I tried to explain that the source of the anxiety is the fact that my body took over two weeks to realize my previous pregnancy had failed. I genuinely thought that I was still pregnant until I started bleeding and they did an ultrasound and told me that my baby had stopped growing weeks ago. 
> 
> She didn't seem to understand and kept telling me that I need to reaffirm to myself when I'm spiraling that I am still pregnant.
> 
> After trying to explain for the third time, I just gave up and did as she told me. It honestly made me feel worse.

**[pbu20-0361]** — Weekly reassuring scans cannot resolve fear when previous missed miscarriage followed normal scans. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> These days I even have feeling my food aversion and nausea is going away, I have nothing to hold on to, I would take suffering over feeling ok just to know that this pregnancy is different than my past one where I experienced mmc, during that pregnancy I had 0 symptoms with perfect numbers and multiple scans with strong heartbeat, last scan at 7w was perfect, baby stopped developing at 8w and I found out at 11w.
> 
> So now Im approaching that 8w mark again, and Im going to weekly scans at this point cause my anxiety is killing me, so far everything was great but it was great in previous pregnancy as well.

**[pbu20-0185]** — Pregnancy anxiety and repeated checking occur without reported prior loss. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I have seemingly no reason to be worried i’ll miscarry or something will go wrong, but it’s all i think about.. checking underwear for spotting/blood… is that a cramp? Is it normal to not feel so nauseous by this point? Am I doing something wrong. Im overweight, but in good health otherwise. Is anyone else in the same boat? excited but dreading your confirmation appointment? happy but worried to be too happy for fear of the worst? how is everyone coping?

**[pbu37-0141]** — Blood-pressure monitoring triggers panic and interferes with toddler care after previous pre-eclampsia. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Anytime I see the numbers go over 150, and higher I’m a crying mess, making everything worse. I’ve already been sent to the hospital and had to get steroid shots in case my baby comes early. It’s hard to even care for my toddler because I’m constantly wired and filled with fear.

**[pbu37-0257]** — Author understands test darkness cannot guarantee viability but continues testing during the wait for care. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I keep taking tests, not because I don’t believe the results, but because I keep hoping to see the second line getting darker and it’s not happening in the way I would have hoped. The second line now appears almost instantly and I don’t have to squint my eyes or tilt the test to see anything, I can clearly see a second dye line. That said, it is still a light second line. 
> 
> Does anyone have any positive stories to share regarding pregnancies where a second darker line never appeared? I know that realistically I should just stop testing (I also have positive clear blue digital), but I think I’m just really struggling with the wait and so scared that a miscarriage has happened. I know a darker test also does not guarantee anything either, I guess I’m just looking for perspective.

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu03-0402] [pbu03-0409] [pbu20-0285] [pbu37-0236] [pbu37-0181]

<a id="pattern-b"></a>

### B. Pregnancy and birth planning also include miscarriage, stillbirth, a surviving twin, funerals and going home without a living baby.

**Belongs in:** §2 (stage arc and first weeks); §6 (grief and fear); §§9–10 (personalisation and preparation); §17.4 (support).

**Relation:** Extends the restored findings; contradicts a baby-arrival-only stage arc.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Make the first two weeks a full section: feeding, sleep, recovery supplies, the partner's shifts, visitors.`

**What should change or remain:** Add a loss-and-bereavement branch rather than presuming every pregnancy reaches newborn care. Include the physical loss, grief after early loss, grief alongside a surviving twin, what to do with gifts and registry items, telling others, support after relatives leave, and whether or when to try again. A pregnancy’s viability can be uncertain while practical preparation is already needed. Funerals and memorials can determine decisions about sweeps, induction, travel, showers and partner leave. A later loss account cannot be counted as a living child or treated as ordinary postpartum disappointment.

**Reading the evidence:** Keep the parents’ actual words about flushing blood, guilt at laughing, not being pregnant without having a baby, and calling one twin dead. Early loss, termination, fetal diagnosis and bereavement are related but are not interchangeable events.

**Evidence (71 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0240]** — Author of “pregnancy loss;”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> She was stillborn at 38weeks, they said she had a hemorrhage and how rare it was that this would ever happen. it is so hard to even type this. our family came out to support us and now everyone left, i haven’t reached out to the groups or anything that they offered and i don’t know if i can, everything happened a week ago.

**[pbu03-0241]** — Author of “pregnancy loss;”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

**[pbu03-0242]** — Author of “pregnancy loss;”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

<a id="pattern-c"></a>

### C. Screening creates a wait for meaning and decisions, often while diagnostic timing conflicts with the time available to act.

**Belongs in:** §4 (options and explanations); §5 (care communication); §6 (questions and fear); §10 Against 6; §17.2 (care navigation).

**Relation:** Extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The decision and care experience need their own treatment.`

**What should change or remain:** Name carrier screening before conception, inconclusive/low-fetal-fraction results, vanished chart entries, diagnostic versus screening questions, amniocentesis timing, twin-specific limitations and fetal MRI timing. The request is sometimes for honesty about disability, life expectancy and termination, sometimes for reassurance while intending to continue. Do not substitute a uniformly positive framing or assume all parents want the same outcome. Retain the speaker’s risk claims as testimony; the share does not establish their accuracy. There are delays even after a person agrees to self-pay.

**Reading the evidence:** The explicit MRI-before-24-weeks conflict and the amnio-result window are decisions with dates, not merely information overload. The accounts of disability and whether a parent could cope belong in the evidence in the words used.

**Evidence (41 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0008]** — Author of “Found out we are both CF carriers and the past few weeks have been so scary and difficult. Waiting on news on baby.”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I feel like total shit I didn’t know we could do carrier screening before getting pregnant. This was a planned pregnancy so I wish I did it.

**[pbu03-0009]** — Author of “Found out we are both CF carriers and the past few weeks have been so scary and difficult. Waiting on news on baby.”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My free moments are so challenging. I’m so worried about her. I’m so mad at myself for creating life that could be at risk for something because I didn’t know I was a carrier sooner. Im so impatient yet terrified of ever seeing the results. I’m so scared of what next steps could be and choices we may have to make. I just want to be able to relax and love her and celebrate her like she deserves. I’m trying to avoid scaring myself with research but I need to be educated on CF.

**[pbu03-0340]** — Author of “Karyotype Animo - No result for T21”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> it's been 2 1/2 weeks. On Thursday, my chart notification went off , so I open it. It was the karyotype portion  , it said "no result " then it said "no chromosome growth " 
> 
> of course I went to Google for help. went  back into my chart, those results disappeared, my assumption is I wasn't supposed to see these results but I did. 

**[pbu03-0341]** — Author of “Karyotype Animo - No result for T21”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My follow up is on July 9th, I guess I have no choice but to wait. I am skeptical in a way due to research I have seen there's actual PPV calculators that give me the same exact number. Yet at the same time I have lost hope. This process is awful. 

**[pbu20-0030]** — Timing of scans, amniocentesis and possible termination feels like a closing decision window. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am also very torn on whether or not to move forward with an amnio. Ideally I’d like to wait until my anatomy scan before making a decision, but that likely won’t be until 20 weeks. At that point, is it too late to schedule an amnio, get the results and still have the option to TFMR if something serious came back?
> 
> I am very conflicted and feel like I’m running out of time to make a decision. I don’t want to do one unnecessarily since so far things are looking good, but I’m also very anxious to miss the window of opportunity if something arises down the road.

**[pbu37-0233]** — Preferred MRI timing conflicts with the reported deadline for considering termination after uncertain brain findings. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> They offered fetal MRI but said they usually prefer to perform it later in pregnancy because the images are more accurate. The problem is that I was also told that if I were to consider termination depending on the MRI findings, I would need to make that decision before 24 weeks. Because of that, I’ve asked if it’s possible to have the MRI scheduled sooner, and I’m currently waiting to hear back.  
> I honestly feel like I’m living in limbo. I love this baby more than anything, but I also want to understand what his future might look like before making any decisions. The uncertainty has been incredibly overwhelming.

**[pbu20-0165]** — Partner sees NIPT as unnecessary expense; author sees preparation for scarce rural services. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I'm in favour of getting it as we live in a small rural area, and a child with special needs requires a lot of preparation, as there is a lack of resources that are very crucial to a child who requires specialized care. My partner says they don't want us to pay for an expensive and unnecessary test just for my peace of mind, but I think that is unreasonable, because it's not about me, but about the baby's wellbeing when they are born. I know it's an expensive test, and times are difficult, but I think it's important to be prepared. Is it unreasonable to get the NIPT even at the absence of more markers?

**[pbu37-0312]** — Author asks for diagnostic honesty after changing anatomy findings and discussion of termination. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> If it’s trisomy like they fear it is my baby does not have any hope of a life and they are saying termination may be best for him and me. I just mostly want to ask if there is any possible way this heart and brain abnormalities aren’t 100% accurate like his fingers ended up being. I just have a hard time believing with so many finding on the ultrasound along with the blood test that it could be wrong but if there is any chance my baby could be ok even if its .001% I have to try. I really can’t afford to lose another baby. I’m not looking for hope, just honesty to make sure I’m not being irrational by thinking my baby doesn’t have a chance. Has anyone ever had multiple abnormalities on an anatomy scan and they magically disappear or be wrong later?

**[pbu37-0200]** — Vanishing twin prevents usable NIPT results and removes expected early reassurance. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We sent the blood work off and everything. It came back Inconclusive. They didn't even run the test at all because they couldn't tell the twins fetal DNA apart. So now we are simply waiting until the anatomy scan to know any chromosomal makeup of our remaining baby. Which will be in another month or so. We do know it's a boy because of a private ultrasound we had done. But other than that we are in the dark entirely on risks for this baby and it is so terrifying that the possibility of knowing early got ripped away from us. I guess I'm looking for support, advice, etc to deal with this anxiety. Thank you ❤️

**[pbu03-0257]** — Author of “Choroid plexus cyst (CPC) was the only flag during the anatomy scan at 19 weeks 5 days - experience with resolving?”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

They said they usually resolve on their own and to wait until the 3rd trimester scan, especially since it isn't paired with other warning signs or flags. (My NIPT tests before this all came back negative for Trisomy variations.) They didn't recommend amniocentesis testing since there is a risk of miscarriage. Would love to hear if anyone else had isolated CPC results and how the long-term outcome was?

> I’ve had 4 miscarriages before this pregnancy, so safe to say I’m an anxious wreck and this doesn’t help. 
> 
> I’m also allergic to penicillin. Has anyone received an alternative antibiotic that was effective? 
> 
> Did antibiotics cause any issues for your baby?

**[pbu37-0165]** — Author questions a proposed preventive stitch despite a reassuring reported cervical measurement. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I have a bicornuate uterus and I am 13 weeks+3days...My NT scan showed cervical length of 3.5cm...My doc is suggesting a cervical stitch since I have a bicornuate uterus irrespective of sufficient cervical length...No other indications...

**[pbu37-0220]** — GD diagnosis triggers blame, online risk fears and restrictive diet changes; risk claims remain the author's. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> Please help me stop my spiral. I’m scared about what this means for baby and am overwhelmed with emotion. I keep thinking, “Why me?” and “What could I have done to prevent this? Should I have fasted for the 1 hour test too?” It also doesn’t help that every doctor’s office does things differently. My old OB’s office makes their patients fast, but mine for some reason did not! I keep going through phases of denial and uncontrollable sobbing and googling. Risks include preeclampsia, stillbirth, induction, a risk of developing type 2 diabetes, and an increase of autism/neurodevelopmental issues in baby. I am beyond concerned and cannot seem to calm down. I have been on a 2 week spiral about this and have made myself sick over it. I even changed my diet but ended up losing 1.5 pounds because I cut out white bread and replaced it with whole wheat bread. I’m terrified to continue the trend of losing weight. I just feel so guilty, like it’s the end of the world, and like I’ve harmed my baby by having this. My sweet baby did nothing to deserve this. I am grieving. I am looking for words of encouragement. I just need to know that everything will be okay, but there’s no way to know that.

**[pbu37-0334]** — Unflagged lab values become alarming through online research while the obstetric office is closed for the weekend. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> The lab didn’t flag it as low, but upon looking up my numbers it’s pretty low. I got the AFP done on its own, not in a “quad” with other tests. I understand it shows a good result for low risk of NT defects, but upon research it says it may be associated with Down syndrome, trisomy 18 or even spontaneous miscarriage. I know my NIPT was clear and I believe more accurate than this, but I cannot shake my fear right now. If not downs or tri 18, why low? That can’t be normal can it? It’s Saturday and my OB hasn’t seen the result yet because it just came in and I can’t contact them till Monday so I’m literally sitting here bawling with my stomach turning.

**[pbu37-0354]** — First-time mother describes receiving a positive SIPS screening call and direction to pursue NIPT. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> hi guys. 24F first time mama here. got a call from my midwife today (bc, canada) saying that my SIPS test screened positive for Downs Syndrome and I must get a NIPT to determine if it is true or false. I am so scared and have cried all day after hearing this.

<a id="pattern-d"></a>

### D. Wanting a baby, planning conception, having support and being pro-choice do not settle whether this pregnancy or parenthood is wanted now.

**Belongs in:** §1 (reader); §2 (pregnancy arc); §6 (ambivalence and fear); §9 (situations); §10 Against 6.

**Relation:** Extends beyond birth-method decisions.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The restored evidence makes induction, pain relief, caesarean choice, consent, recovery and previous trauma central to what women ask to understand.`

**What should change or remain:** Include whether to continue a pregnancy, abortion, adoption and parenting without a partner as current pregnancy experiences. Independence, lifestyle, career, school, health, existing children and grief can enter the decision with or without financial hardship. Pro-choice authors can personally want to continue; intentionally pregnant authors can want termination. Some distinguish both options as grief, some are clear in their decision, some miss appointments or let time decide. A late dating discovery can remove an option the author expected. Record reported local abortion restrictions and procedures as the author’s circumstances, not verified law or a recommended action.

**Reading the evidence:** A decision workbook is actually used in one account; it is not evidence of a Mamivio purchase. The source saying an unanaesthetised procedure influenced the decision needs its full context. Do not collapse disagreement, a jointly difficult decision and explicit coercion into the same relationship category.

**Evidence (90 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu20-0024]** — Planned conception prompts uncertainty about whether desire was socially driven. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> now that I’m pregnant, I am truly terrified, and I don’t know if I need to have an abortion. I can’t separate if my desire for a baby was purely ‘societal‘ in the sense that I got carried away with my friends, wanting what they wanted or what they have (babies now).

**[pbu20-0025]** — Seeking accounts from independent travellers who continued a planned pregnancy despite ambivalence. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> are there any extremely independent people out there who had the same roller coaster experience finding out they were pregnant, after planning it? and what I can’t find much on reddit: after the rollercoaster, did you have your baby and it was the best thing you ever did?   
> 
> 
> anyone with similar worries to me had children and have good perspectives to share? any travel lovers that have made it work with young kids?

**[pbu03-0156]** — Author of “TW termination because of difficult temperament baby”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Both my husband and I are beyond exhausted and even if er wanted a big family we decided with a heavy heart to be one and done. Then plot twist - I’m pregnant, which is wild to me, given it took us two rounds with IVF to convince the our daughter, and I’m on the pill.

**[pbu03-0157]** — Author of “TW termination because of difficult temperament baby”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My husband believes it’s a miracle, and wants the pregnancy to progress. I’m one the fence. I don’t know if I’m even the slightest capable to care for one more child, given that I already give my first my all every waking second of my day. I’m stretched beyond thin, I’m already having a hard time keeping up with basic hygiene for myself. I’m only 7w but I’m constantly nauseous and fatigued. Now it’s summertime where I live and we (me and the baby) can spend a lot of time in the garden. I don’t know how to keep her entertained come winter, and me being pregnant and unable to carry her places.

**[pbu20-0242]** — Pregnancy after neonatal loss and breakup creates attachment, grief and practical uncertainty. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> On one hand I feel emotionally attached to this pregnancy already and I deeply want to be a mother again. I worry I would experience significant grief if I chose not to continue, and I feel very connected to this pregnancy already.
> 
> On the other hand, the father and I are not together and there is significant trust broken. I worry about co-parenting dynamics and instability. I worry about whether I can realistically provide the life I want for a child right now. I grew up with a single mom who loved me deeply and was emotionally present for me, but my dad was not consistently involved.
> 
> I am in a very emotionally vulnerable state after recent loss. I also find myself grieving the idea of a supported pregnancy experience with a partner who is emotionally present, which I do not currently have.
> 
> I guess what I’m struggling with most is figuring out how to make a decision like this when both options come with grief, uncertainty, and no clear “right” feeling.

**[pbu37-0263]** — Author distinguishes emotional readiness and lifestyle timing from financial security, relationship stability and family help. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> The biggest reason we’re considering terminating isn’t financial or relationship-related. We both have stable jobs, live together in NYC, and know we want to spend our lives together. We also haven’t told either of our families yet, but we’re certain they would want us to keep the pregnancy and would be incredibly supportive. We know we’d have a strong village to help us if we became parents.
> 
> The difficult part is that neither of us feels ready to leave the stage of life we’re in. Right now, our lives revolve around friends, hobbies, and travel. We see friends several times a week, travel 5–6 times a year, and none of our close friends are planning to have kids anytime soon. We know becoming parents would change our priorities and lifestyle much sooner than we expected. At the same time, we’re confident that if we chose to keep the pregnancy, we would step up and be loving, responsible parents.

**[pbu20-0313]** — Reported local abortion procedure and restrictions influence reconsideration and planned partner conversation. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Where I am they require an ultrasound prior to being able to make an abortion appt, the soonest appt was just past my 8 week mark. The only abortion option is a sharp d&c with no anesthesia option, Id have ibuprofen once in a recovery room, and im required to be at the hospital for 48 hours after the procedures with no visitors allowed. I'm not in the US but a remote location with only the one clinic option. I'm terrified of having it since i spoke with the doctor about the procedure and have my ultrasound coming up in less than 2 weeks. In this time i have thought about the pregnancy and the abortion every single day. Slowly I've wondered if itd be possible for having a conversation and discussing if he would be open to other options outside the sharp d&c. I would never force him to be a father but wanted to present it as an option. Saying "i love you so much but I dont think I can go through with the abortion. As much as i want to be with you I understand you never wanted children so I'm prepared to raise the child in my own so if you do not want this, it's okay" because essentially its my choice to have the baby but its also his choice to be a father. I can't take his choice away from him or force him to do something he doesnt want. But im nervous to have the conversation. Can I get some opinions? Am I going crazy from the horomones wanting to go against abortion when I was fine with it before talking to the doctor?

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu03-0058] [pbu20-0157] [pbu20-0348] [pbu37-0340] [pbu37-0341] [pbu03-0129]

<a id="pattern-e"></a>

### E. Some partner and family problems involve sexual assault, reproductive coercion, threats and violence rather than an unread book or unequal chores.

**Belongs in:** §5 (partners and relatives); §9 (support and confidentiality); §10 For 6; §17.7 (pressure-to-end exclusion).

**Relation:** Extends the restored coercion gap; contradicts mental load as a sufficient description.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `From her side, the gap is mental load more than absence:`

**What should change or remain:** Replace that summary with a range that explicitly includes ignored requests for contraception, ejaculation without consent, secretly removed condoms, pressure to end or continue pregnancy, threats to leave or abandon existing children, sexual assault during illness, intimidation, property destruction and physical violence. Some authors describe being unable to leave or obtain care because of transport, money or the approaching birth. Others do leave and describe hospital staff, a social worker or a domestic-abuse team helping. Partner-sharing and partner education cannot be the default response when the partner is the reported source of harm. Retain allegations as attributed accounts and distinguish them from independent findings about the other person.

**Reading the evidence:** Pressure operates in both directions. A doctor and relatives reportedly urge abortion on age/marital-status grounds; other partners threaten abandonment if the author has an abortion. A female partner is the reported aggressor in one account. None should be lost in a universal male-support-person model.

**Evidence (77 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0372]** — Author of “Argument with bf”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I didn't want to be pregnant but my bf didn't put on protection at the time that I requested it and now I am "accidentally" pregnant. I didn't have access to emergency contraceptive or abortion care because I lost my license due to my illness and was dependent on him for transportation. I couldn't ask anyone else and he begged me to keep the baby.

**[pbu03-0491]** — Author of “So frustrated with my husband (but honestly more so myself)”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> A month and a half later, he ejaculated inside me again after I'd explicitly asked him not to because I didn't want to risk pregnancy. I had to rush and get the morning after pill but I still became pregnant.

**[pbu37-0107]** — Author reports a partner secretly abandoning agreed condoms, followed by unwanted pregnancy and abortion. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> The reason I left was because we had agreed to use condoms while having sex, I didn’t want to get on birth control yet cuz I wanted my body to heal more. Well we started to have sex again and he was using them. Then one time he snuck in not using one and got me pregnant. I swear I had no idea he didn’t have one on! If he did I would have stopped him! I had to go get an abortion because I was absolutely not ready for another pregnancy.

**[pbu03-0436]** — Author of “Rant about my husband forcing me to do things I don't want to do while pregnant.”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My husband is also pressuring me to do unnecessary medical interventions, because my in-laws think it is necessary due to my husband's sibling's child having an ultra-rare birth defect that would only be detectable through whole-genome sequencing. All of our testing so far has come back great and relatively low-risk, so there is no medical indication for a CVS or amniocentesis. I even did an expanded NIPT (maternity genome) because my husband pressured me and said he would feel better not doing whole genome sequencing if the test came back ok. After a low-risk expanded NIPT, he's still pressuring me to do a CVS to get more testing done, with my in-laws leading the charge behind the scenes. Under no circumstances am I doing an unnecessary CVS. I do not want to electively put a needle in my belly for no good reason. There is a good chance the genome sequencing, which is very novel, will find "variants of unknown significance" or mutations that are confined to the placenta, which will lead to us needing to decide if we do an amnio and/or if we want to abort this baby that I went through hell and back to get pregnant with and love so very much. And it will be all for nothing. I don't want to create unnecessary pain, risk, and stress because someone else thinks it's a good idea. It's my body, and my pregnancy. And for the record, there's nothing wrong with my baby.

<a id="pattern-f"></a>

### F. Pregnancy itself can bring disabling depression, rage, dissociation, self-harm and suicidal thoughts even when the baby is wanted.

**Belongs in:** §2 (symptoms and stage arc); §6 (fear, shame and distress); §9 (previous experience); §10 (scope of support).

**Relation:** Extends the severity, mechanism and disclosure barriers.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The restored words include panic, sadness, lack of excitement, body distress, regret and fear of not bonding or surviving birth.`

**What should change or remain:** Name suicidal thoughts, attempts, self-harm, violent rage, feeling detached from reality and considering abortion because continuing pregnancy feels intolerable. Do not translate these into ordinary tiredness or lack of gratitude. An IVF pregnancy can be followed by wishing for miscarriage; another author explicitly says life is loved but exhaustion makes it impossible to function. Planned pregnancy and previous successful treatment do not guarantee the same mental-health course. Some authors are already medicated, in therapy or under a crisis team and still waiting or struggling. A referral or medication mention is not an outcome.

**Reading the evidence:** Disclosure has consequences in the author’s mind: fear of judgement, child-protection involvement, hospitalisation, upsetting a partner or admitting regret. One author lies to a psychiatrist about self-harm. These are mechanisms for silence while care exists, not simply a missing contact list.

**Evidence (54 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0058]** — Author of “I am all over the place”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am one month post FET and have gotten a positive test and I spend 90% of my time regretting this and wishing I miscarry or experiencing a form of claustrophobia. I wanted this, went through about 3 months of IVF process and instead of being positive and grateful I am grieving and wishing I could run away from my current life and start a new one child free, husbandfree and in seclusion. I am prone to depression as is and the hormones of pregnancy have brought me to mental pits I had not visited in almost a decade.

**[pbu03-0374]** — Author of “Feeling suicidal from this level of fatigue”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

I can’t do anything I want and be present at all with my daughter. I push through most of the time but this is no way of life. Lately, it’s feeling like I can’t even push through anymore and I’ve just been having suicidal thoughts.

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu20-0064] [pbu03-0152] [pbu37-0135] [pbu37-0158] [pbu03-0059] [pbu37-0045]

<a id="pattern-g"></a>

### G. Severe postpartum distress can persist with extensive support, and fear for the baby or fear of disclosure can block treatment and rest.

**Belongs in:** §2 (recovery, feeding, sleep and bonding); §5 (partners seeking help); §6 (shame and disclosure); §17.4 (support end dates).

**Relation:** Extends; contradicts treating absence or the end of practical help as a sufficient explanation.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.`

**What should change or remain:** Add the specific accounts of suicidal thoughts, self-harm, hearing voices, paranoia, rage toward a partner, urges to shake a baby without acting on them, compulsive contamination precautions and inability to sleep when someone else has the baby. A second-time parent describes financial stability, daycare, a supportive husband, five weeks of maternal help and a year of leave alongside severe depression. Keep the later report of improvement after medication had time to work. A partner asks how to obtain help as a non-patient when treatment itself is feared. One screener account reports pressure to change an honest self-harm answer to “never”; do not treat a recorded screen as proof that the person felt safe to answer.

**Reading the evidence:** Describe the actions without assigning new diagnoses. Where the record reports a diagnosis, prescription or change, attribute it. Do not recode all distress as anger at a baby: some authors explicitly locate rage only toward a partner, while others describe unwanted thoughts about the baby.

**Evidence (37 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0103]** — Author of “Wife is 4 weeks postpartum and her anxiety about germs/contamination is getting extreme. How do I get her help?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `partner`).

> She is terrified of anything potentially being “dirty” around the baby. If his pacifier touches basically anything besides his mouth, she considers it contaminated and gets a new one. We can easily go through 15 pacifiers in a day.

**[pbu03-0104]** — Author of “Wife is 4 weeks postpartum and her anxiety about germs/contamination is getting extreme. How do I get her help?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `partner`).

> Every single time she pumps, she washes her nipples with Dawn dish soap first because she is worried about contamination. They’re now so dry and irritated that they sometimes bleed. When that happens, she throws away the pumped milk because she’s afraid there could be “HIV blood” in it.

**[pbu03-0105]** — Author of “Wife is 4 weeks postpartum and her anxiety about germs/contamination is getting extreme. How do I get her help?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `partner`).

> She was prescribed Zoloft, but she refuses to take it because she’s afraid it will get into her breast milk and hurt the baby, even though she isn’t currently giving him the breast milk anyway.

**[pbu03-0106]** — Author of “Wife is 4 weeks postpartum and her anxiety about germs/contamination is getting extreme. How do I get her help?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `partner`).

> And probably my biggest question: **What do I do if she doesn’t recognize how severe this has become or refuses treatment because the treatment itself becomes another thing she’s afraid could hurt the baby?**

**[pbu03-0107]** — Author of “Wife is 4 weeks postpartum and her anxiety about germs/contamination is getting extreme. How do I get her help?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `partner`).

> I know it’s the weekend so the hospital will be my only choice until Monday, but I’ve never had to call on behalf of an adult before. Will they even listen to my concerns since I’m not the patient?

**[pbu03-0150]** — Author of “Scared to take Zoloft for PPD/PPA”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I had GAD before pregnancy but felt fine during pregnancy. I had so much support and no financial or relationship issues, I didn’t think id be the one to suffer from it. Well plot twist, my Dr diagnosed me with PPD/PPA and said to get on Zoloft.

**[pbu03-0171]** — Author of “Guilt and shame over post Partum rage at husband”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My mom left yesterday after a month so possibly something to do with that. I ended up storming out to the lounge and slamming the door. Then came back to the room and tried to ask him to leave and not speak to me and go sleep in the other room but he kept talking and I flew into a rage. I grabbed his trousers off the floor and threw them across the room and down the corridor multiple times. I bashed my fists against my husband 😭 and then went to his cupboard and ripped all his clothes out on the hangers and threw them across the room.

**[pbu03-0172]** — Author of “Guilt and shame over post Partum rage at husband”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m too ashamed to seek help from my health visitor or GP as I’m scared of what they’ll say and having this follow me for the rest of my life. The guilt I feel is immense. I have absolutely no rage against my baby she is so perfect. It’s just directed at my husband. He is also perfect and has done absolutely everything, especially the first two weeks when I was bed ridden and couldn’t do anything for myself except feed the baby.

**[pbu20-0123]** — Regret and diagnosed depression occur despite practical support and a year of leave. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> I have a 6 week old. She’s my second baby. My first is 4 years old now. I have a supportive husband, had my mom with us for 5 weeks helping with the new baby. We are financially stable. I’m 33 years old. I have a full year of maternity leave. My son is in daycare full time. We genuinely wanted a second baby and it also made sense to have a second with our situation. Now I’m regretting it so so so much. I can’t stop crying. The only time I can stop is when I sleep. I was diagnosed with ppd and I’ve been taking Zoloft for 2 weeks now. Although i started with a low dose of 25mg and am working my way up to 100.

**[pbu20-0124]** — Reply challenges expectation that experience and support would make second postpartum easier. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Honestly I don’t care. I also know as a second time mom this will pass. I know it will. I had similar feelings with my first but it wasn’t this intense. It all passed and my son is the light of my life. I just don’t understand why I feel this way since it’s my second time and I have all the support possible and the meds. I thought this time would be easier but my doctor told me because I felt similar with my first and I had PPA, I was more at risk of developing mental health issues this time. I hate that I can’t get to enjoy the newborn phase yet once again.

**[pbu20-0125]** — October reply reports substantial improvement after medication took time. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> I’m feeling sooo much better! My meds took their time to work but then one day something just clicked and I started feeling 1000 times better. Please seek help if it lasts more than 2 weeks ❤️

**[pbu37-0099]** — Author reports being pressured to change an honest prenatal self-harm screener answer. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> The first time I did it during my pregnancy, I answered the questions honestly. The last question asks if you think of hurting yourself and the choices are: often, sometimes, hardly ever, and never. I put "hardly ever" because that's true. Everyone basically bullied me into changing my answer to "never." The nurse literally said: "you put 'hardly ever,' but you meant 'never,' right?" Until I just got angry and said "yeah, if thats what it has to be." And then they just kept pressuring me to confirm i wouldnt hurt myself.

**[pbu37-0069]** — At three weeks postpartum, author reports crying, feeding difficulty, voices and paranoia before a scheduled assessment. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Things got worse, I started hearing voices and I grew extremely paranoid I was being watched or that I could see faces all the time. That settled a little, but not enough for me to feel safe again.

**[pbu37-0101]** — Parent reports urges to yell or shake, denies wanting harm, and describes aggressive rocking followed by guilt; baby's age unspecified. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

There will be times where im rocking her to sleep and do it aggressively because im so angry and all she does is laugh because she thinks im playing with her and it makes me feel terrible.

**[pbu03-0151]** — Author of “Scared to take Zoloft for PPD/PPA”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

**[pbu03-0152]** — Author of “I’m not okay”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

I asked him to take me to the hospital that day for suicidal ideation and to prevent me from harming myself and our son because I didn’t want to. I just wasn’t okay.

**[pbu03-0153]** — Author of “I’m not okay”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Before we got pregnant we talked about the high risk of my becoming depressed during and after pregnancy, he’s seen my depressive episodes before we chose to get pregnant. He said he understood and would support me as best he could. I did everything I could to setup support for myself: I joined a once weekly moms group, had a family support specialist come out once a week to check in with us to offer us support as parents and touch on milestones for our son, and in addition have been attending a 12 step group for adult children of alcoholics and dysfunctional families for 4 years this August. I have amazing friends with healthy boundaries who offer wonderful support as friends and have even babysat a few times here and there to give me breaks. I did everything I could to support myself for the possibility of depression from this.

**[pbu20-0062]** — At three months, describes depression beginning immediately postpartum and mixed response to treatment. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

I was too depressed to eat or sleep and barely had enough energy to take care of my baby. At one point, I walked out of the house in the middle of the night until I reached the edge of a highway, hoping that if I got hit it would all be over, but I did not follow through with it. I am on medication now and in therapy, but I’m not sure if it’s helping. Some days I’m okay, but I often find myself totally enraged when my baby won’t stop crying, to the point I want to hurt myself and don’t want to be here anymore so it will all be over. I usually just end up sobbing while caring for LO for the rest of the day instead.

**[pbu20-0065]** — With a seven-week-old and older children, even seeking therapy becomes another logistical task. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

And what was that name of the therapy group that works with my husband’s insurance? (I have thought about going for a while now so I have looked into it). I could text his coworker (who I am close friends with) and ask her. 
> 
> I was almost laughing that I was thinking about scheduling a therapy session because I couldn’t relax, but then I was also not relaxing because I was thinking about the logistics of getting into therapy instead of just enjoying the damn massage 😂😵‍💫🙃

**[pbu20-0070]** — Partner and NICU updates helped author through self-blame and suicidal thoughts. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I failed to protect my son, for a second there I thought about suicide but then my husband would get me out the bed, out the house so we can see the baby in the NICU and my baby was so so strong, they kept telling me all the good things about him. They told me he’s a fighter for sure and not just because of what we were going through but he HATES diaper changes and to be fair he still does hate diaper changes but he’d fight every nurse in there now he doesn’t fight me as much. The hospital sent me letters explaining my son’s treatment, what he’s doing and he was doing such a wonderful job and I knew if I would’ve died I would’ve missed seeing my wonderful baby boy grow up and I didn’t want to miss that.

**[pbu20-0080]** — Anxiety prevents sleep even with a partner doing other care tasks. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My partner is obviously doing everything but feeding. I know people will say 'sleep when baby sleeps' during the day but I literally can't. I get so worked up and anxious about everything that I can't settle down. I've tried meditation, breathing etc and it hasn't worked at all. As a result, I just let my partner sleep because I don't see the point when straight after a change, baby wants the breast instantly.

**[pbu20-0083]** — Author feels guilty accepting help despite sleep deprivation and long-running feeding problems. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I haven’t slept one wink since 2 days ago.. and my mental health is really low, its not his fault hes just as tired as me but wow, what a journey, i haven’t had a happy baby since the day he was born bless him, i feel like such a failure, to top it all off i live with my partner but feel like a single parent most of the time, i love him dearly and i do feel loved by him, he tries his very best after work but he deserves a break too, 12 hour shifts and then looking after a CMPA baby, it’s not fair so i try to take it on all on my own, my mam helps me when she can but it just makes me feel even worse as he’s my child, i should be able to look after him, i feel so much guilt for feeling so misrable all the time, i just want to cry and did for weeks but i feel like i have no tears left, am i beating myself up?

**[pbu20-0087]** — Reply describes medication concerns grounded in previous negative or absent effects. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> No I'm not opposed to it I just have a slight fear that certain medications might affect me like other psychiatric medications have affected me in the past, usually very negatively or had no impact at all. But if I could find something that works for me I would be really happy about that.

**[pbu20-0130]** — Months later, author reports numbness rather than expected flashbacks after premature birth. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I thought that after going through trauma, I would get flashbacks, and nightmares, and be triggered, maybe cry a lot or even have panic attacks. I’ve never really dealt with mental health issues before. Well, I don’t really have any of those symptoms, so it should be all good, right? Instead, I feel kind of numb, and detached, and overall blah. Like not really interested in doing anything. I barely care about anything. I don’t really feel like playing with my kids. I have a few hobbies I try to distract myself with. When my mind wanders, it always goes back to my pregnancy, birth, and NICU stay. There are some moments that just stick with me and I can remember exactly what I was thinking and feeling at the time.

**[pbu37-0064]** — Mother describes screaming awakenings since birth and their continuing effect on her own sleep. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

I don’t even mind the broken sleep anymore, but waking up to screaming has me constantly on edge, and I’m convinced it’s why I feel like I can’t catch up on sleep.

**[pbu37-0067]** — After a difficult pregnancy, fear of partner mistakes prevents delegating newborn care. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

**[pbu37-0068]** — An available sleep shift fails because anxiety drives monitoring and taking over. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

**[pbu37-0070]** — Isolation, exhaustion and household conditions overwhelm a parent awaiting perinatal mental-health assessment. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I just can't cope anymore.
> 
>  I know things would get better, but I can't keep pushing through to get to that point. We have no help. I have no help. I don't know what I'm supposed to do anymore. I just want to sleep. I grew up in a home that was messy and dirty, my childhood bedroom had a mould problem and I didn't learn how to take care of myself, or how to clean until I was an adult. I feel like I'm putting my baby in that neglect already and I hate myself for it. We've given her one bath in her three weeks. I can't wake up and do another day again. I had our baby in a good routine, but I'm doing everything alone. I just can't do this.

**[pbu37-0075]** — At seven weeks, practical prenatal daycare decisions become separation anxiety and guilt. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> When I was pregnant it was so easy, moms gotta work, dads gotta work, baby goes to daycare… whatever, fine. Now that he’s here I can’t stand the thought of leaving my baby boy with complete strangers that are taking care of a bunch of other little babies that are going to bring their germs in there with them…. I cry every time I think about it and I also feel this INTENSE guilt. I’m his mom, I’m supposed to be caring for him all the time! The thought of potentially missing milestone with him makes me so sad and what if he’s uncomfortable or unhappy there?

**[pbu37-0103]** — Ten months postpartum, author recalls escalating aversion to a dog beginning during pregnancy. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I hate my dog. I know I shouldn’t and I’ve made desperate attempts to fix the way I feel, but it’s just gotten progressively worse since my pregnancy. It went from her being annoying, to “I love her, I just don’t like her”, to I feel deep rage and hatred toward her.

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu20-0123] [pbu20-0124] [pbu20-0125] [pbu37-0067] [pbu37-0068] [pbu20-0163] [pbu37-0283]

<a id="pattern-h"></a>

### H. Symptoms can remove the ability to eat, stand, sleep, study, work or care for a child, and the promised trimester relief may never arrive.

**Belongs in:** §2 (symptoms and daily function); §3 (stock reassurance); §4 (attempts at relief); §§8–9 (work and existing children).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The broken week-by-week script includes persistent pain, exhaustion, vomiting and incontinence, repeated remedies and disrupted sleep.`

**What should change or remain:** Retain the functional scenes: stopping school because vomiting persists despite Zofran, being unable to finish a short shopping trip after sleeping all night, pain while standing to wash dishes, chronic pain despite multiple treatments, urinary retention, persistent infection and restricted movement. Add concurrent serious illness, including breast-cancer surgery during pregnancy while toddler care continues. Effects of thyroid disease, anaemia, reflux, nausea and medication side effects overlap in the accounts; do not diagnose the cause from the quote. Symptoms can be both unbearable and reassuring to someone frightened of loss.

**Reading the evidence:** Keep treatment successes and failures together. Some describe helpful reflux treatment or recovery; others exhaust remedies or find the remedy creates another functional problem. The speaker wanting no more pregnancy can still want the baby strongly.

**Evidence (83 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0031]** — Author of “Dealing with loneliness and depression since getting pregnant”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> After about 6 weeks I became so incredibly sick I couldn’t keep water down and would throw up multiple times a day even on zofran. I could no longer go to school and had to drop out and if you have ever went to cosmetology school you know they are unforgiving when it comes to missing hours.

**[pbu03-0331]** — Author of “Anyone have a herniated disc while pregnant?”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Before pregnancy I relied on NSAIDs, but those are no longer an option. I’ve had two transforaminal injections that didn’t take. Sitting longer than 30 minutes is extremely painful. I’m laying awake in bed now at 3am in pain. I’m trying pool therapy, PT (although there doesn’t seem to be a lot that’s working), recently started acupuncture, Tylenol, and low dose Gabapentin.

**[pbu03-0332]** — Author of “Anyone have a herniated disc while pregnant?”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I keep getting told to be patient and it takes time, and avoid surgery at all costs, but feeling so severely debilitated in my day to day life that the thought of waiting longer is hard. I’m also terrified what pregnancy will do to it. Feeling desperate.

**[pbu03-0479]** — Author of “Absolutely exhausted at only 5 weeks!”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Today I slept 8-9 hours, got up to go shopping (by car, and I wasn't even driving) and when we arrived I was so tired we just took turn and came back home. I then slept from 13 to 15, and I can fall asleep again any time again.

And no, I don’t like other sugar-free options. They’re disgusting. I’m just over it. I’m ready to be done. lol and don’t give me that bullshit if you’re gonna look back and remember and miss being pregnant no I’m not gonna look back and remember and miss being pregnant I’m gonna look back and say thank God I survived.

**[pbu20-0301]** — Reassuring tests and supportive team do not remove severe first-trimester symptoms. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

So I know there’s nothing seriously wrong, but I’m just so sick. I’m exhausted all the time, I feel sick whenever I eat. I just need some advice on how to deal with this and I suppose some reassurance that it does get better.

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu03-0032] [pbu20-0283] [pbu20-0295] [pbu37-0013] [pbu37-0352] [pbu03-0374] [pbu03-0375]

<a id="pattern-i"></a>

### I. Medication and preparation have to fit a person’s mental health, neurodivergence, trauma and ability to function, with competing risks as they understand them.

**Belongs in:** §2 (symptoms); §4 (pain relief); §5 (care navigation); §6 (fear); §§9–10 (personalisation).

**Relation:** Extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Trimester, pregnancy number and a calendar are not sufficient personalisation by themselves.`

**What should change or remain:** Add ADHD medication needed for daily tasks; autism and selective eating; disability-related referrals made without discussion; ongoing addiction treatment after sobriety; previous trauma; eating-disorder history; medication discontinuation and body changes; and contradictory prescribing instructions. Pain relief can be fully effective yet provoke panic through immobility and claustrophobia. Anti-nausea medicine can help but stop work, or taste so bad it causes nausea. A helpful reframe is also reported: medicine enabling nutrition and reducing the stress of sickness. These are reported experiences and questions, not evidence that one medication is safe, unsafe or indicated.

**Reading the evidence:** Do not erase medication names, doses, substance-treatment history or sexual trauma. Preserve source errors rather than silently correcting them in quotations; the analysis must not turn a speaker’s number or belief into an instruction.

**Evidence (30 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0385]** — Author of “The hospital was kind of awful?!?! TW difficult birth”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> It really sucks because it was 100% effective for the pain. But the anxiety wasn’t worth it. Once I got into the OR and the surgery was underway, I wasn’t thinking about my legs anymore and was obviously thankful I couldn’t feel anything. And when I was in the recovery room and I started to feel my legs again, my anxiety melted away.

**[pbu03-0386]** — Author of “The hospital was kind of awful?!?! TW difficult birth”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I do have sexual trauma and claustrophobia, and I can see how that might cause the reaction I had to it!!!!

**[pbu03-0493]** — Author of “microdosing GLP”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Realistically, what will an OB do with this information? I have a lot of medical trauma and am terrified of being judged for micro-dosing on my own (I had gone through a reputable online provider). 

**[pbu03-0494]** — Author of “microdosing GLP”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My OB already made a comment about how she doesn’t approve of GLPs and sees them as an “easy way out,” which makes me nervous about disclosing. 

**[pbu20-0173]** — Reply explains need for ADHD treatment to function while seeking pregnancy-specific advice. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> This makes me feel better! I’ll have to get a more in depth opinion from my OB but I have to be able to take some sort of adhd med if they don’t approve the vyvanse. I have moderate to severe adhd and cannot execute daily tasks without it 🥲

**[pbu20-0246]** — Author explicitly wants experiences matching autism, ADHD, depression and limited support. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> This worries me, especially with a baby on the way. I don’t want to terminate the pregnancy, but I am scared about my capacity to handle what is coming.  
> I have very limited patience and currently very little internal space for another person, because even taking care of myself is already difficult.
> 
> Another important factor is that we don’t really have a support network. We will be doing most things on our own, with very little family help.
> 
> I can’t tell yet what is autism/ADHD, what is depression, what is situational stress, or what is just everything combined.
> 
> I guess I’m looking for people who recognize themselves in this: autistic and/or ADHD people who became parents while feeling overwhelmed or afraid of it. How did it go for you? What helped? What do you wish you had known beforehand? Please give me some hope

**[pbu20-0345]** — Prescribed addiction treatment evokes shame and fear of newborn monitoring and child services. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> It’s really frustrating bc I thought I was done with this chapter of my life and that I’d be able to have a pregnancy without this medication. I feel like a failure bc of it and like I’m a terrible mother for it. I know that my baby will have to be monitored for NAS and that it will be my fault for it, and even though it could be harder drugs like I used prior to getting sober I just can’t help but feel like a POS for it. I keep stressing myself out about how my baby will be when he gets here and if I’ve royally screwed him up, I worry about having childrens services involved even though i’m apart from this medicine that on paper I’ve done everything else correctly as far as my health, diet, doctors appointments, getting everything that baby and I will need, etc.

**[pbu20-0365]** — Reply identifies prescribed treatment and contrasts sobriety with distress about continuing medication. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> it’s Medication Assisted Treatment- i’ve been prescribed suboxone (switched to subutex during pregnancy) for the last year and a half to aide my issues with opioid dependency. prior to being the better, healthier person i am now i made very poor decisions and fell into a cycle of pain medicine addiction. I’ve been sober for a little over 2 years now and still somehow am on this medicine:,)

**[pbu37-0013]** — Several nausea treatments tried; the most effective leaves the author unable to work. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0027]** — Selective eating complicates pregnancy nutrition despite access to food. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0085]** — Unexplained testing, conflicting risk labels and referrals without discussion undermine trust. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

My midwife basically introduced herself by saying my BMI was too high for a water birth because no one would ever be able to lift me - I'm 5'7 and am really athletic but my BMI is technically obese. I have shared these concerns with my midwife only to have it hand waved away and told sorry won't happen again, but it keeps happening!

I am also on progesterone which could be why I’m so bloated but getting on the scale I’m up a number of pounds and I’m at 10 weeks. I read that you don’t gain much during the first trimester but I’m consistently bloated, retaining water and just feel not myself. I know it’s part of the journey but was hoping I could get insight! 
> 
> Sorry if this is not the right forum. I was extremely fit beforehand and would train 5-6 times a week with weights so it’s just a bit hard with the adjustment.

**[pbu37-0174]** — Author weighs debilitating preventive antiviral side effects against concerns around vaginal birth. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> my OB put me on acyclovir for preventative measures since i have a history of hsv1 (genitally). i RARELY get outbreaks and haven’t had one my entire pregnancy. basically, this medication is making me feel super ill. on top of regular pregnancy symptoms at 38 weeks, i now feel like i have the flu everyday :/ my OB doesn’t have a solution and she’s very risk-adversed. did anyone not take this medication and have a healthy vaginal birth? of course if i felt ANYTHING down there, ill opt for what’s best for my baby.

**[pbu37-0199]** — Zofran helps severe nausea, but dissolving-tablet flavour makes the author feel ill. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Tried a bunch of things before finally caving and taking the zofran. And of course it's a pill you need to dissolve on your tongue. And they made it taste like bubblegum and chemicals. I've taken it for 2 days now and it's helping so much, but taking it makes me feel so ill for a good half hour because of the taste!

**[pbu37-0217]** — Author questions preventive aspirin based on age despite two previous uncomplicated pregnancies. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> LONG STORY LONG: Anyone else over 35 prescribed baby aspirin daily JUST because of age with no other risk factors? Obviously will do what is best for baby, but don’t want to take something (especially a NSAID!!!!!) if it could actually hurt baby in any way.

**[pbu37-0255]** — After losses, author fears both clotting and taking Lovenox; suspected causality is the author's question. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> Either way I can’t help but wonder if lovenox had something to do with it considering I started the shots a week before everything was starting to fuse. I have said this to my doctors and they all look at me like i’m crazy and I understand millions of women take lovenox with their pregnancies but the question still is why did this happen to ME? I started taking them last week for this pregnancy just because I am worried about a blood clot but also worried that it’ll happen again. Was it the chicken or the egg basically🥴I have read some Reddit users posts about starting lovenox as soon as they find out they’re pregnant and sometimes even before getting pregnant and they claim it helped sustain their pregannt after recurrent loss. My husband is convinced it was just a lighting strike type of event and it won’t happen again. Maybe it’s the mom guilt or the “what ifs” speaking but just trying to hear some other stories and have some comfort while I wait to see what happens with this pregnancy.

<a id="pattern-j"></a>

### J. Knowing whom to call does not ensure being heard, getting the correct result, obtaining treatment or being transferred between services.

**Belongs in:** §5 (being believed and follow-up); §6 (who to ask); §§16.2 and 17.2 (care navigation).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends the limits of a contacts page.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Care navigation includes failed referrals and follow-up, dismissive or blaming treatment, unexplained results and conflicting professionals.`

**What should change or remain:** Add concrete failure points: silence promised to mean normal results when it did not; a refill challenged despite consultant notes; a possible molar pregnancy first seen in a chart; contradictory verbal/written heartbeat findings; a lost-pregnancy test marked duplicate; a spontaneous conception dropped by the fertility clinic before an OB is arranged; an unconnected fax delaying paid testing; and rejected or delayed appointments. Some authors become reluctant to return after dismissal. Contrary cases show a maternity team escalating unfamiliar exposure concerns, a former OB offering review and a private GP recognising urgency. Information can name the process, but cannot make a practice accept the patient or respond.

**Reading the evidence:** The author already following discharge instructions can feel punished for reporting precisely those symptoms. Some explicitly want an explanation or a treatment direction, rather than another generic instruction to ask the doctor.

**Evidence (75 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0113]** — Author of “39 weeks - very scared.”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Referred to hospital for torch screening.  maternity said they would not make contact if all was fine in five days. They didn’t make contact. 
> 
> All was not fine. Went in for my antenatal check today at 39 weeks, and learned I have CMV infection.

**[pbu03-0313]** — Author of “Feeling upset, scared and completely unheard after L&D visit”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> She then said “your blood pressures are on the high end of normal, we can’t deliver for that. We have a 37 weeker next door who was just intubated. Your baby is fine where she’s at. I’m not gonna deliver when nothing is wrong.” She was so rude about it, I was taken aback.

**[pbu03-0314]** — Author of “Feeling upset, scared and completely unheard after L&D visit”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> OB comes back into room and says “we’re keeping you overnight so we can monitor more and show you that you don’t have preeclampsia. If your BPs stay high all night we’ll induce in the morning”. I said if my proteins are good, and you think this is just anxiety (🙄) and baby looks great, I don’t want to be admitted. You were just telling me about a baby the same age as mine who is doing horribly. Then she backtracks and says that’s really rare and 37 weeks is term (which I know- but you were literally just trying to guilt me using a sick baby before you decided you wanted me to stay.)       
> I made sure I wouldn’t be considered AMA for serious concerns and she reiterated that it would just be monitoring and that baby is doing excellent, so I discharged. 

**[pbu03-0315]** — Author of “Feeling upset, scared and completely unheard after L&D visit”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> Don’t tell me to report symptoms if I’m going to be talked to like I’m crazy when I mention them. Don’t tell me my blood pressures are high and you may want to induce early if they’re fine. Now I feel like unless I’m positive I’m in labor, I don’t even want to go near that place. But they made sure to give me discharge instructions “call if you experience headache that won’t go away, dizziness, blurred vision” etc, all the things I told my OB that got me sent to L&D. Would love for the communication between my OB and L&D to be clearer.

**[pbu03-0092]** — Author of “Ectopic Pregnancy Ruptured (uncommon warning signs!)”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Last night I had another acute session. Tried to get up from bed at 2AM and passed out. Called the ambulance who said it wasn’t an emergency and I should make my own way to the hospital. Didn’t want to go to the Emergency Department at 2AM with a small baby, so took some pain meds and slept until 7. At 8 I tried getting a GP call/appointment at my local NHS surgery. They kept asking if I had shoulder pain or nausea/vomiting - which I didn’t. Deemed not an emergency.

**[pbu03-0093]** — Author of “Ectopic Pregnancy Ruptured (uncommon warning signs!)”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Finally at 10 am I paid for a private GP appointment. (At this stage worries that it’s maybe something with my intestines as the pain is shooting to my butt.) GP tells me for the first time that he’s worried it could be an ectopic pregnancy or ruptured cyst and sends me to the ER.

**[pbu03-0094]** — Author of “Ectopic Pregnancy Ruptured (uncommon warning signs!)”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Finally received a scan at 5pm. All hell breaks loose. Confirmed ectopic pregnancy in my fallopian tube that has burst at 5 weeks. They see internal bleeding. Rush me to surgery, explaining they will remove my tube. Also tells me other organs might get damaged (FREAKING OUT at this stage. All alone as husband is at home with baby and no family nearby.) When they try and take some pre-surgery bloods I pass out.

**[pbu20-0102]** — Author repeatedly has to justify consultant-prescribed medication to receptionist. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> So I need a refill of my prescription and my notes say from my consultant for my GPto just refill the prescription for me to pick up. So I call the practice and a receptionist picks up and we go through details and she said okay let me find it and whilst doing it she’s asking what it is and why I need it. I explain I’ve to take my tablets everyday so I can avoid a transfusion and I need them everyday for them to work. She asks who told you that? I said the Obstetrician consultant who wrote in my notes for me to have them and said the gp will be able to refill them for me. Also then proceeded to ask why I think I need them. I again explained I’m almost in my third trimester and need the tablets to help with heart palpitations and energy levels etc for my pregnancy. She keeps asking me who told me to take them and I explain. Eventually she says she’s checking the system then the phone cuts dead. Luckily I was able to make an appointment with a gp for tomorrow through the online triage system. I just hope I can get my medication or unless I’ll be bothering triage for a refill with a 6 hour wait because they didn’t read the notes on the system.

**[pbu20-0208]** — Possible molar pregnancy appears in notes without explanation; child's pediatrician intervenes. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Well next day I'm sitting waiting for my toddler's doctor to come in to our appointment and decide to see if I can get any more info from my after visit notes. That's when I see a note saying "possible partial molar pregnancy." This was NEVER said to me during the appointment. I immediately started crying and panicking. My daughter's pediatrician came in and asked what was wrong. I explained, and she looked horrified. She said that is not how that should be handled, that I should have been told, and that I definitely should be having my HCG monitored over the next few weeks. She told me to call them back and tell them what she told me.
