<a id="pattern-k"></a>

### K. Birth preferences and consent depend on pain, prior loss, safety fears, available support and how options are explained in the moment.

**Belongs in:** §4 (birth decisions, pain relief and consent); §6 (birth fear); §10 Against 6.

**Relation:** Confirms and extends; contradicts flexibility as a complete explanation.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `A flexible plan helps some people, but the earlier flexibility conclusion cannot explain every outcome or place responsibility for traumatic care on the woman.`

**What should change or remain:** Keep refusing, accepting and changing interventions visible. One Foley insertion continues despite pleading to stop. Another author accepts induction after a stillbirth warning and still feels regret; the new passages explain the decision rather than cancelling the regret. A pregnant trans dad reports unsafe-feeling relatives and doulas, anaesthetic allergies and a proposed friend-on-call arrangement. A general recommendation to bring a partner or hire a doula is not usable for every person. Others weigh induction after loss, VBAC restrictions, fear of examinations, timing after complications and whether natural labour is worth a risk as they understand it.

**Reading the evidence:** Quoted medical risk estimates and accounts of what a hospital will allow remain attributed claims. The quotations preserve the author’s terms about “natural,” “synthetic,” consent and being unable to say no; the digest does not adopt those terms as clinical conclusions.

**Evidence (39 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0042]** — Author describes pregnancy, birth or recovery in the archived post. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I got no medication to help. I hear of people getting stuff or even epidurals and believe me I want one. I went from a firm closed cervix to them forcing it in me. Getting it in was so traumatic I was screaming and crying with pain. Everything went so fast I didn’t even feel I could say no I asked them to stop and I couldn’t do it but they baby talked me and kept going. I didn’t feel I had totally consented. I was pleading with them to stop

The nurse said yeah it’s labor it sucks you should have scheduled a c section. If you reaaalllyy want morphine we arent really even supposed to give it when can give it to you but I kinda need this room sooo….

I always heard people say not to elect for a c section because recovery is so much worse but this recovery does not at all compare to what I experienced.

**[pbu20-0141]** — Conflicting clinician opinions leave mother uncertain about VBAC after short interval. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I’ve had one doctor tell me absolutely no to a VBAC and another doctor tell me I was a good candidate and 7 months was enough time for my uterus to heal. So I’m over here debating on what to do. I guess I’ll just leave it up to the high risk doctor that I’ll be seeing tomorrow.

**[pbu20-0142]** — Prior failed induction shapes decision about induction for current high-risk pregnancy. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

But I’m scared about having another failed induction! Has anyone had an induction after already having a previous c-section? How did it go?

**[pbu20-0214]** — Pressure for induction conflicts with previous difficult induction and desire for spontaneous labour. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Telling me how my placenta will go bad, I have extremely high risk for  pre-eclampsia, still birth etc and seem to get upset when I tell them “I am not against induction if movement with the baby changes, my blood pressure even spikes a little etc, I’d just like to wait and see how things go naturally “. I am not completely against induction and I was induced with my first pregnancy due to my blood pressure getting high at the very end (she was still born on her due date) but the induction was rough and I was in labor for two full days! I know going natural doesn’t guarantee a faster or smoother labor/birth, but I was really hoping to naturally go full term and see if labor would begin on its own. I’m just nervous I could be making the wrong decision that could put my baby and me at higher risk just because I want to experience it happening naturally and not going through induction again!

**[pbu20-0240]** — Reports of unnecessary caesareans shape uncertainty about birth setting. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0111]** — Author cites online mockery of pre-parenting intentions and epidural preferences. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0171]** — Pelvic health physiotherapist feels professional fraudulence and guilt about repeat caesarean; infant-health concern is her belief. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

I’ve had people say to me “Oh, Im so sorry” when I say I’m having a repeat c. like it doesn’t count or I’ve failed.

**[pbu37-0175]** — Author weighs spontaneous labour against timing and reassurance after a previous seventeen-week loss. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

I also don’t want to wait until 40+ weeks anyway due to nervousness. He’s currently head down and will likely stay that way. <a id="pattern-l"></a>

### L. Surviving birth with a healthy baby does not resolve maternal trauma, memory loss, separation or a partner’s fear about the mother.

**Belongs in:** §2 (birth and first weeks); §4 (birth experiences); §5 (partners); §6 (fear and identity).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `A healthy baby or an outwardly successful birth does not settle what the mother experienced; relief and improvement also appear.`

**What should change or remain:** Add forgotten first meetings and the practical request for photographs and repeated accounts from the support person; numbness rather than recognisable flashbacks; a debrief first offered after a fourth difficult birth; and partners told the baby is healthy but left uninformed about the mother. A grateful account of life-saving care can still contain grief, confusion and failure feelings. NICU separation and general anaesthesia can remove expected holding/feeding scenes. Distinguish the actual outcome, the speaker’s appraisal and the later processing rather than labelling every intervention a trauma or every successful rescue a satisfactory experience.

**Reading the evidence:** Keep chronology where the same post supplies both a smooth-delivery account and later memory loss. These are coexisting parts of one account, not independent votes for opposite birth methods.

**Evidence (24 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0018]** — Author of “Placenta Previa, Feeling Like a Failure (illogical)”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I know I should just be feeling so grateful to be here, with my baby, and for now, we are safe. But I am just starting to feel the walls close in, I so so so don’t want to have a super preterm baby, and a c section. They made it clear because of the placenta previa it’s a much more complicated procedure than a standard section, and I will likely be under GA, and not allowed to hold the baby before it goes to the NICU.

**[pbu03-0019]** — Author of “Placenta Previa, Feeling Like a Failure (illogical)”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

My first birth experience with my now 3.5 year old was traumatic, and he sustained injuries from the instruments that caused him to end up in the NICU for days. Breastfeeding was a nightmare but I persevered. I really wanted this journey to go better, but it feels like the harder I try the worse things get.

<a id="pattern-m"></a>

### M. Respectful care, effective pain relief, positive induction and easier recovery remain real parts of the same evidence.

**Belongs in:** §4 (range of birth experiences); §2 (recovery); §§16.4 and 17.6 (contrary evidence).

**Relation:** Confirms; counter-examples to a uniformly traumatic or progressively harder account.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The restored accounts include successful and difficult inductions, epidurals, unmedicated births and caesareans, changed preferences and regret in both directions.`

**What should change or remain:** Keep these accounts alongside the difficult ones, with the same level of verbatim detail. Include a nurse asking before every touch, an epidural called worthwhile after unmedicated labour, a planned section easier than anticipated, a second recovery much easier than the first, strong bonding after induction, less anxiety after a miscarriage, a reassuring scan after repeated losses and a parent valuing care even when feared complications happened. These accounts establish the range; they do not establish frequency or a treatment effect.

**Reading the evidence:** A long labour or an intervention is not sufficient to infer the author’s appraisal. Explicit happiness, relief, gratitude and confidence must remain audible rather than appearing as a token closing sentence.

**Evidence (28 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0072]** — Author of “Choosing a c-section”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> The section itself was quick, smooth and I felt next to nothing. Yes, the experience is a little strange and of course there are moments in your recovery that might be difficult. But there is no easy way to deliver a baby into this world. I think the recovery was actually easier than I expected. The first couple of days you can’t really do much but I was never really in lots of pain.

**[pbu03-0161]** — Author of “PSA to all the first time moms who got delt a difficult baby.”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Yes! That was my experience also, my second one was much easier right out of the womb. The first one literally came out with his elbow and gave me deep laceration all the way to my anus, so you can also imagine my postpartum healing, but my second postpartum was waaaaay easier and almost pain free.

**[pbu03-0403]** — Author of “listen to your gut. i knew i had preeclampsia.”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> it was surprisingly very smooth! once i had some good pain management it went very smoothly and i pushed for literally only like 30 minutes ahaha and he just rocketed out! he's now in the NICU gaining some weight and learning to eat by mouth instead of a tube then he can come home. just had him passed out on my chest doing skin to skin and that was the best feeling ever😌

**[pbu20-0017]** — Induction and epidural coexist with a calm, intentional experience. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> So they started me on the pitocin with my plan being to get the epidural when my husband got back. Induction started just after 3pm. My phone died and I didn’t have my charger so I had no distractions from labor. They turned the pitocin up from 1 to 3 to 5 and then to 7 all before I even felt like I wanted the epidural.

**[pbu20-0018]** — Positive second birth accompanies early bonding and calm after previous postpartum depression. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> And I think it’s affected my postpartum, in the best way. I feel so deeply bonded to my daughter, to both my children. Hormones have come and gone in their regular waves, my milk has come in, and we are adjusting to the nights of broken sleep and feeds and snuggles. It’s all been so easy to ride. I’ve had no anxiety, no depressive feelings, I feel like the best version of myself. I feel more human than I have in my entire life, and I love being a mother more than any role I’ve ever had.

**[pbu20-0159]** — After thirty hours of unmedicated labour, author chooses walking epidural. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I had wanted to attempt an unmedicated labor, and for the first 30 hours or so, I did just that! It was… intense! I’m sure the synthetic prostaglandins did me no favors. Counter pressure, a fitness ball and a warm shower are what got me through dilation from 1cm-8cm. I was making sounds akin to whale singing during the worst of my contractions lol 
> 
> But when it was time for us to go to the delivery room, facing some of the worst pain I’d ever felt in my life along with the possibility that my birth could end up in a C-section if I wasn’t progressing… I decided to get the walking epidural.
> 
> It was amazing.

**[pbu20-0160]** — Author judges epidural worthwhile despite needle-site discomfort. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Yes I do still feel a bit of discomfort in the entry point of the needle for the epidural. Honestly though, it was worth it. I couldn’t have taken the oxytocin without the support of the epidural.

**[pbu20-0220]** — Contrary account: feared complications occurred, but author describes trust in staff and acceptable outcome. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> During delivery, everything I was scared of happened- baby's heart rate went down, had episiotomy, internal tear, vacuum delivery, no epidural, hemorrhage, high BP and HR - however nothing mattered because you survive it and trust the medical staff that are there. Everything works out the most important thing for me was that my baby was ok.

**[pbu37-0249]** — Author praises repeated consent, comfort measures and rapid access to an epidural. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My nurse (an absolute angel!!) was so very helpful! I’ve never felt more cared for in my life. She asked before she did ANYTHING and usually nurses or anything were not fond of asking if they could do something. (Well that was before I switched to my current doctors..) Everytime she would have to touch my belly she would ask, she helped me take a nice bath/shower when my contractions worsened, and she helped me breathe through contractions until I couldn’t take it anymore. I planned to go without any medication other than Pitocin, and whatever else went along with that. But I also wanted to keep my options open. So after pushing through for a period of time, I asked for the epidural. She immediately hopped on to getting me an epidural. I have no regrets, that epidural had me chill out so so quickly, and I felt amazing lol, I would be a great sales person for the epidural I loved it so much😭

**[pbu37-0131]** — Author celebrates an anatomy scan with no reported concerns after four losses. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> After 4 miscarriages, my husband and I just left our 20W anatomy scan. Baby girl was absolutely perfect and measuring a little ahead of due date. No concerns at all.

**[pbu37-0181]** — After missed miscarriage, author reports excitement and less anxiety despite acknowledging line darkness cannot establish viability. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I know darkness of lines can’t determine the health or viability but something just feels different this time. Last time, I couldn’t put my finger on it but something just felt off even from the first positive pregnancy test. But this one feels different in a positive way and I actually feel super excited and way less anxious this time!

**[pbu03-0073]** — Author of “Choosing a c-section”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> It was more twisting to pick up the baby or leaning over which is best to avoid, so there will be a time where you’ll need your partner to pass baby to you. I pretty much stayed sat down or laying down for first few days so my husband had to bring everything to me anyway!

**[pbu03-0159]** — Author of “PSA to all the first time moms who got delt a difficult baby.”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> My birth story you may ask? Perfect, got the epidural just like I wanted, he latched immediately after birth and after three days of hospital stay we brought him home. The first 2 months were blisfull, is this what everyone was talking about?! I LOVE BEING A MOM, GIVE ME 10 MORE! He never actually slept at night but at first he woke up 2-3 times a night, with a little bit of fussiness at 4AM where he needed about an hour to go back to sleep and then he would sleep until 9AM or so. Both me and my husband are MDs so I was semi ready for the night from my job. My husband went back to usual work grind after a month of paternal leave. There was some anxiety on night when I was alone with him but nothing I couldn't handle.

**[pbu03-0229]** — Author of “baby is here!!”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I was in labor for about 24 hours and actively pushing for 3 hours and made no progress. they had told me baby pooped inside me and I was scared about that, and she told me since baby couldn’t move down the birth canal I had to get a c section and honestly this was one of the scariest things in my life bc also my doctor said since I kept pushing his head had got a little swollen. I cried so hard, but we made it through! Me and him are okay, healthy… and I can’t wait to spend all of my time with him! 

**[pbu20-0058]** — Team's explanations of planned birth and NICU resources provide reassurance. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> It has helped that the high risk OB team has been so understanding and sweet. They make sure to give me positive information (baby at 34 weeks should do really well, etc) and they have told me that planned c sections are a lot calmer than what happened last time. They reassure me that I'm at the biggest hospital in the state (with a level 3 NICU)  and a level 4 NICU is next door at the children's hospital if needed.

**[pbu20-0161]** — Author reports reflux relief after OTC failures and prescribed pantoprazole. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> So I have had WICKEDDD acid reflux my entire pregnancy, that just seems to get worse throughout 😭. I’ve tried allll the OTC medications I could. Gaviscon liquid antacid was the only thing that somewhat helped, but sometimes it made me throw up if the reflux was too bad. 
> 
> I finally brought it up to my OB that it was becoming unbearable, and she recommended pantoprazole, a prescription in the proton pump inhibitor family. It’s pregnancy safe!!! 🙌🙌 of course, they want you to exhaust all other options before trying it, but I genuinely was at the end of my rope with the acid reflux. 
> 
> This stuff is a literal god send! First day I took it, I had almost NO acid reflux!! It’s been four days now, and it’s truly helped so much.

**[pbu20-0183]** — Beyond existing fear quote: detailed birth plan accounts for seizure triggers and fatigue. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m being induced at 37 weeks and there’s a huge plan in place. They’re so professional about it and have told me being induced at 37 weeks can be a long process and I will be monitored throughout in hospital - pessary then (likely) oxytocin drip.   
> An epidural is being given pretty much immediately, removing stress and pain (which can cause seizures).  
> If labour goes beyond a certain time to the point where I’m starting to become very tired (another trigger), a c-section will be done instead.

**[pbu20-0219]** — Looking back, worst-case research felt preparatory but consumed happiness. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> In my head if I knew the worse case scenario that would prepare me for the reality in case that scenario became real. However this took so much from me, took away so much of my happiness and only by the end I let myself get excited about this baby and "relax". This to say - if you think something is wrong absolutely go get checked and trust the doctors and professionals, but if you are like me and have an awful lot anxiety - do try to think rationally and enjoy it! Right now I feel so bad that I took away the good experience that I could've had.

**[pbu20-0348]** — Author describes joy after unexpected positive test and discovering thirteen-week pregnancy. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I told my boyfriend, he screamed out of joy. Anyways, I'm 13 weeks! Ftm' or anyone, Any tips? I am so glad, and so excited that I now have a human inside of me.

**[pbu20-0367]** — Unquoted ending of duplicate crosspost reports a healthy birth after rapid reconception. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Thankfully, our rainbow baby came at 37 weeks on the dot. Healthy and the perfect, most happy baby who is now 15 months.

**[pbu37-0082]** — Pregnancy initially goes well despite a history of anxiety and depression. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I have had such a great pregnancy. As I have struggled with anxiety and depression in the past and am currently still on medication for it I was worried it would rear its head in pregnancy and I’d struggle. I have been completely fine despite the usual anxious feelings leading up to scans.

**[pbu37-0120]** — Third pregnancy brings perspective and a positive adjustment despite newborn chaos. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `partner`).

> We're in the new born trenches but I'm glad to be back because I know it doesn't last forever and time goes by so quick (even though it doesn't feel like that sometimes), baby boy has slotted into the chaos and the big sister has already stepped in her role.

**[pbu37-0149]** — Follow-up reports improvement after partner acknowledges behaviour and takes responsibility. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We talked, and he finally acknowledged his behavior and admitted he had been handling his stress very poorly. He’s not really open to therapy, so that’s not an option for now.
> 
> Things aren’t perfect, but they are better. He’s making an effort, and the fact that he took accountability for his mistakes really made a difference to me.

**[pbu37-0206]** — Author credits a small rural hospital with saving both lives. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am forever thankful for the care that my tiny, rural hospital was able to provide. They truly saved my baby’s life, as well as my own.

**[pbu37-0213]** — Follow-up scan reveals a heartbeat after an inconclusive early scan and prolonged worry. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I still remember my first transvaginal ultrasound. Nothing much could be seen at that time, and I was told that it might simply be too early. Since then, every day felt so long. I prayed every night, worried about every symptom, and wondered if everything was okay with our little one.
> 
> Today, during my follow-up ultrasound, my heart was racing. I prayed silently before the scan started, asking God for strength and for good news. Then came the moment I had been waiting for.
> 
> There was our baby. 🤍
> 
> Not only could we finally see our little miracle, but we were also blessed to hear and see a strong heartbeat. The tears I had been holding back immediately fell. In that moment, all the waiting, worrying, and praying felt worth it.

**[pbu37-0222]** — Author deliberately allows happiness after loss despite the expectation to keep early pregnancy quiet. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> Today I tested positive for pregnancy and am 4weeks5days so SUPER early! I have miscarried before, so know not to get too excited, but I don’t want to go through this pregnancy denying myself happiness because of a what if.   
> Keeping it quiet from everyone is so hard! And I’m so aware it’s at least 7weeks until we can share the news. But I had to tell someone so I’m telling you guys!   
> I’m so excited to be a FTM 🥹

**[pbu37-0228]** — Mother describes a long induction and pushing as worth it while admiring her newborn. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Had my baby girl in the afternoon via induction as I have GD. 
> 
> It’s 2am and I can’t help but stare at her, she’s literally perfect.
> 
> Had her via vaginal birth and it was a LONG process and a 2 1/2 hour push time but totally worth it.   Just coming here to say don’t be discouraged if you’re feeling discouraged during delivery.  It’s all worth it in the end!

**[pbu37-0353]** — After infertility and loss, author remembers pregnancy-long happiness and the expectation that a baby would complete their lives. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> When I finally got pregnant, I was over the moon. Even though the pregnancy itself was difficult and physically rough, I spent the entire nine months buzzing with happiness. I was so excited. I thought having a baby would complete our lives.

<a id="pattern-n"></a>

### N. Recovery can make holding, feeding, walking or sitting impossible, even with the expected supplies and people present.

**Belongs in:** §2 (recovery); §4 (postpartum preparation); §6 (what is normal); §§9–10 (capacity and support).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Recovery is not just a supplies checklist;`

**What should change or remain:** Add the functional detail behind incapacity: an open breast wound packed by a partner after abscess surgery, ongoing bleeding before retained tissue is treated, joint/back pain beginning in labour, a second tear or section recovery different from the first, and pads, sprays, ice and cushions failing to permit sitting or holding the baby. One parent with severely injured arms fears not being able to hold the baby at all. A parent’s account of “normal postpartum” can be the explanation that delayed their request for treatment. Retain improvement after treatment and formula feeding in the same abscess account.

**Reading the evidence:** Do not use degree-of-tear labels or a familiar operation name as a measure of tolerability. The speaker with a second-degree tear describes inability to hold the baby; the speaker with a second elective section describes far more pain than the first.

**Evidence (19 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu20-0033]** — Mastitis symptoms worsen despite repeated antibiotic advice. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Then a week later I woke up, had a bad feed session and the lump seemed worse so I went to the ER. I had a suspicion that I had an abscess but I figured it was paranoia. At the ER, they gave me a prescription for antibiotics and instructions to continue icing and increase the ibuprofen. Two days went by and I was worse so I contacted my OB who prescribed me a different antibiotic. I was sobbing in pain and felt awful. I was missing out on so much with my daughter from pain and being sick. I literally cried on the phone to different nurses I felt so horrible.

**[pbu20-0034]** — Abscess surgery leaves an open wound requiring partner and nursing care. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I was admitted to the hospital for IV antibiotics and to receive surgery on the abscess. It was drained and washed out while I was under general anesthesia. Surgery had to leave the wound open for healing so now my husband packs gauze into my boob hole once a day. I also get seen by a home health care nurse 3x a week for wound care. The situation sucks beyond words but I'm optimistic that I'm finally on the right path for healing.

**[pbu20-0035]** — Reply describes mistaking infection symptoms for normal postpartum changes. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> There's so much going on postpartum it's hard to determine what's normal. I thought body aches were hormonal but it was the infection. It's always better to reach out and I wish I had taken myself more seriously so I could advocate for treatment sooner.

**[pbu20-0036]** — Reply reports recovery and switching to formula on doctor's recommendation. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I'm starting to feel better and am finally getting to spend more time with my daughter. She's an angel and worth the pain. We did move to formula feeding at the recommendation of my doctor but she's been perfectly happy with it. I'm excited to put this behind me and be the mother I always wanted to be! Not one that has to lay in bed all day with pain and illness.

**[pbu03-0147]** — Author of “Running on fumes”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I had an elective CS for the second time almost 2 weeks ago. The first recovery was a breeze but this time around it is legit the most painful physical experience I have had in my life. Every day I feel pain from a seemingly novel part of my body. My chest, upper, mid, and lower back, pelvis, incision site, groin, thighs, all hurt and it's been 11 days. I won't even get started on contraction pain - those have me doubled over screaming internally several times an hour. I'm dependent on NSAID suppositories to function atp. I have no appetite most times and I'm extremely depressed. My mental health was not the best to start with but now ​​I can't remember feeling true happiness anymore.

**[pbu20-0127]** — At eleven months, describes disabling pain beginning during labour. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m dealing with chronic back pain from si joint dysfunction onset at labor (and I mean debilitating pain, like I cannot pick my baby up or walk for more than a mile or sit for more than 30 minutes) this has been the most pain and the hardest physical journey of my life. I hope to be better one day but it has been a struggle Daily. For months the pain was so constant and so severe I would cry and cry and pray and just take muscle relaxers to function. Now it is getting much better but I am still in pain a lot and functionally very limited.

**[pbu20-0254]** — Arm injuries during pregnancy create fear of being unable to hold baby. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I broke both of my forearms and almost died from blood loss. I’ve had multiple surgeries, screws and plates placed in both arms, and pins put into my right arm. I still have little to no feeling in parts of my left hand, and I have almost no range of motion in either arm or hand.  
> For a while, I couldn’t even feed myself. I couldn’t brush my own hair. I couldn’t even wipe my own butt without help, which is one of the most humbling and heartbreaking experiences I’ve ever gone through.  
> One of my doctors even told me there was a chance I might not be able to hold my baby the way I’d always imagined. Hearing those words completely broke me.

**[pbu37-0230]** — Three days after a second-degree tear, pain prevents walking, sitting or holding the baby despite multiple measures. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I delivered my baby 3 days ago, and ended up with a second degree tear, the moment the epidural wore off i was in the most pain ive ever been in my entire life, it hurts so badly that i cant breathe normally while i try to walk to the restroom, nor can i walk normally from how swollen i am down there, the pull ups and pads make me miserable due to anything touching me down there feels like sandpaper,  
> Witch hazel/postpartum spray/icing , i cant even hold my baby, sit in a chair, lean on my side, im genuinely getting extremely exhausted and depressed, ive tried a donut pillow as well/ and a soft cushion, im so miserable and i cant help but sob over it each night or any time i try to get up,  
> Has anyone else experienced this? How long until your life went back to normal, im losing hope

**[pbu37-0204]** — After emergency surgery, author cannot yet process feeding and needs sleep. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> The nurse asked how my son was doing and everything was good with him. She asked me if I felt like sitting up or feeding him yet. I couldn’t really make sense of anything yet, so I said “I want sleep.” She told me that it’s okay to go back to sleep for a little bit.

**[pbu03-0089]** — Author of “Any new(ish) moms still feel their epidural spot/ruminate on traumatic birth?”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Within a week of being home, I was back at the emergency room because my 4th degree tear exposed my urethra. It was awful. I was in so much pain.

**[pbu03-0101]** — Author of “New mom”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Right now it feels like we're trying to recover emotionally, physically, and financially all at once.

**[pbu03-0102]** — Author of “Joint / Ankle Pain”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am 15 months PP and I’ve had ankle pain since giving birth but it seems to only get worse. I was active before pregnancy, active during pregnancy and active now. It’s getting harder everyday bc the pain is awful.

**[pbu03-0170]** — Author of “Guilt and shame over post Partum rage at husband”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m 6weeks PP - traumatic birth and recovery and I’m still in pain and healing so lots of pent up energy and frustrations with being almost housebound and feeling exhausted if I try do anything.

**[pbu03-0173]** — Author of “Constipation while EBF a baby with food intolerances”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Can i use Movicol? I have been constipated since giving birth 3 months ago and i have some very bad fissures so i am getting desperate now but i don’t want to hurt my baby. I have tried prune juice and increasing fluids with no improvement.

**[pbu20-0117]** — Haemorrhage from deep vaginal tears requires surgery and transfusion after VBAC. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Due to being unable to see well, they rolled me to the OR and eventually found 2 long sulcal tears (deep tears along the sides and top corners of the vaginal wall). These are apparently very difficult to manage because they are hard to see, and there is a ton of blood flow to that area. It took them needing the help of a urogynecologist, over 2 hours of time, lots of stitches, a clotting agent tons of surgical packing in the canal, and 2 units of blood plus fluids and blood pressure medicine to get me stable. I lost over 2.5 L of blood.

**[pbu20-0118]** — Recovery and feeding are affected by birth trauma while caring for older children. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> That being said, I feel so run down on top of being in pain from all the tearing. I’m trying to breastfeed but understandably my body just went through trauma so I can’t produce as much as I need right now. It’s all just a lot. I’m blessed to have my mom and husband helping so much. We have 3 other kiddos so that is an added amount of physical and mental exertion.

**[pbu20-0119]** — Author describes persistent distressing physical symptoms since childbirth; postpartum timing unspecified. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Since giving birth, I’ve been dealing with a constant 24/7 arousal feeling that never goes away. It is not sexual desire. It is a persistent physical sensation that is extremely distressing and exhausting. Along with that, I also have bladder symptoms, including a constant urge to urinate and a feeling of pressure or irritation in that area.

**[pbu20-0126]** — Looking back from eighteen months: emergency procedure for retained placental tissue. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> When I was 2 weeks post partum, I had to have an emergency D&C for retained placenta. They removed a 7cm by 8cm piece of necrotic tissue that was fused to my uterus.

**[pbu37-0098]** — Continuous bleeding since birth leads to delayed ultrasound and a retained-placenta procedure at eleven weeks. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Just had to go under anesthesia for a D&C to remove a small piece of placenta that was causing my constant non stop bleeding. I have not had a single day where I haven’t bled after I gave birth to my baby on 1/21. At my 6 week OB appointment, I mentioned I was still bleeding heavy with cramping. She asked me if it could be my period, but having PCOS I have irregular cycles and I wasn’t sure. I told her my bleeding hasn’t slowed or stopped at all so I didn’t think it was. She ordered a pelvic ultrasound which I had to wait 2 weeks for. So I was 8 weeks postpartum by the time I got the ultrasound, still bleeding with clots. Confirmed that I had a 2 cm long “retained tissue” and they scheduled a D&C for 3 weeks out.

<a id="pattern-o"></a>

### O. Feeding can be painful, traumatic, identity-defining and incompatible with sleep, while imposed plans or equipment shortages add pressure.

**Belongs in:** §2 (feeding and recovery); §4 (preparation); §5 (staff and relatives); §6 (shame and choice).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.`

**What should change or remain:** Add sexual trauma activated by breastfeeding, feeling predatory while trying to feed, nipples damaged by repeated latching or washing, formula and reflux changes that help or worsen the situation, and fear that formula removes the mother’s purpose. A parent is touched without permission and blamed for not pumping when no pump was available. Pumping can take time away from the baby it is intended to nourish. Another asks about formula to preserve mental health and sleep. Keep cost pressure and judgement alongside physical pain; do not describe perseverance, exclusive breastfeeding or formula as a universal resolution.

**Reading the evidence:** The competing preferences are explicit: some desperately want breastfeeding to work, others want permission to share feeds or stop, and one formula change produces a happier baby in the author’s account. The source claims about reflux, milk allergy and medicines are not adjudicated here.

**Evidence (21 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0015]** — Author of “pregnant with my second and looking to succeed in breastfeeding this time. (TW: mentions of sexual abuse)”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I unfortunately found out when i first tried breast-feeding my son, that I have a lot of sexual trauma attached to my breasts, which made it super hard for me to breast-feed him. I felt uncomfortable doing it, and for some reason, I felt as if I was a predator if I tried to force him to feed on my boob. I know it's not rational, and I know it's not real, but unfortunately, sexual trauma affects you in the worst ways!

**[pbu03-0016]** — Author of “pregnant with my second and looking to succeed in breastfeeding this time. (TW: mentions of sexual abuse)”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My question is this, how soon after delivery do you start breast-feeding? Do you try popping them on the boob as soon as they're out? Do you wait a few hours? What did you guys do?

**[pbu03-0017]** — Author of “pregnant with my second and looking to succeed in breastfeeding this time. (TW: mentions of sexual abuse)”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I felt like an absolute failure with my son, because I wanted to breast-feed, but ultimately I couldn't. i want to establish a good supply, and i want to breast feed for at least 6 months, but the goal is 12. also, formula in this day and age is not cheap, and i'd like to minimize how much i'll need (if any) thank you! 

**[pbu03-0109]** — Author of “Baby started failing to thrive in doctors care..”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Reflux got worse immediately even though she wasn’t really drinking a lot more. She became extremely unhappy, couldn’t be on her back without her mouth filling with liquid, arched in pain all the time, was honestly such a sad baby girl at 1 month old. Then every time we went back, her growth curve would lower a bit.. then came the reflux medication. Which didn’t really help. We couldn’t do tummy time, back time on a play mat, she just slept, or squirmed in pain for hours. She barely gained weight for a month. It got to the point she screamed in pain if I put her down, elevated, on her side, it didn’t matter. I sat up right for 7 hours while she slept. I was absolutely losing my mind. I was watching my baby fail to thrive. She went from the 55th% to the 32%.

**[pbu03-0110]** — Author of “Baby started failing to thrive in doctors care..”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> We switched her back to cow formula, and it’s like we have a different baby. We still have reflux, but not nearly as bad, she plays, she smiles, she doesn’t cry in pain anymore, she’s going through a growth spurt and we can tell she’s gaining weight already.

**[pbu20-0079]** — Five days postpartum, severe feeding pain and sleep loss overwhelm recovery. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> The last few days since we came back from hospital I have had no more than 4 hrs sleep. We're EBF currently and baby is in a day/night confusion and cluster feeding. When she cluster feeds, its agony when she latches on because I am switching between breasts every hour. I'm talking like full body sweating/tensing, grimacing, hair pulling type pain. In order to cope, I've literally scratched myself multiple times or botten my nails to the point of bleeding just to distract me from the pain.

**[pbu20-0085]** — Author associates inability to breastfeed with loss of maternal role. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> After the magnesium I stopped making colostrum even though I was making a ton before, I kept trying to breastfeed but he wasn't gaining weight so he's on formula now. I just feel so inadequate as a mother, I feel like he doesn't need me anymore because if anyone can feed him then what am I really good for?

**[pbu20-0086]** — Unexpected formula costs add to pain, sleep loss and fear of discharge. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I've been crying a lot for the past two days and my incision really hurts now but it's like I can't stop once I start. I'm still in the hospital right now and I just don't know what I'm going to do. I already told WIC I was going to breastfeed and now I literally can't and I wasn't expecting to have to buy formula and now I'm worried about the financial aspect of it all. I feel like I'm not doing enough even though I only get about 4 hours of sleep a day. We're supposed to be discharged later today and I'm dreading it.

**[pbu20-0223]** — Loss-specialist counsellor suggests formula to protect sleep; author fears judgment. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> One thing that has come up is formula vs. breast feeding. The counselor suggested considering formula for me, noting that my mental health has taken a beating and my mental health is also closely tied to my sleep.
> 
> I see her point, and I do know my husband will help a lot (he has extensive experience in childcare). But, I’m still nervous about making this choice and dealing with judgement from others. Has anyone here gone through similar things? If so, how did you think about this decision? Thank you!

**[pbu37-0266]** — Mother reports intrusive breastfeeding handling and blame for not pumping when no pump was available. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Trying to learn to breast feed him In the nursery pulling the curtain closed in pain constantly three or four people walking in telling me what to do, at one point the one nurse walked in grabbed my tit and shoved it in my sons mouth, who I’m still trying to figure out how to hold in the first place, he’s tiny  
> Ask for a pump they don’t have any left just for a nurse to be like you should have been pumping this whole time

**[pbu37-0267]** — Hours of pumping feel like time lost with the baby after a difficult hospital stay. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Finally get him home pumping hours at a time trying to get my breast milk in more feeling like I’m not spending the time with him I’m supposed to be

**[pbu03-0108]** — Author of “Baby started failing to thrive in doctors care..”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> As soon as he saw her, barely doing a check on her, he said CMPA. He put her on Nutramigan, told me her reflux would get worse because she’d want to drink more, and sent me on my way. He told me cradle cap on her eyebrows was eczema, and that the congestion and reflux are two different symptoms, and all would be fixed on this formula in 2 weeks id see a difference.

**[pbu03-0167]** — Author of “How do you face the day after a really bad night?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I’m exclusively pumping and have been since about a week and a half pp. Because she was so small, hospital staff threatened us with nicu stay if she didn’t eat and she and I weren’t able to figure out breastfeeding in the hospital or when we got home. She was exclusively on formula for the first week and a half before I tried pumping. I’m still working on building my supply so I don’t have any spare milk around. I’m able to pump what she needs for the next feeding and occasionally I still have to supplement with formula.

**[pbu03-0168]** — Author of “How do you face the day after a really bad night?”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> So far, everything was going alright until I went to put the lid on the milk… I bumped the top and it fell to the ground. There goes almost 4 oz of milk onto the carpet. And she starts crying just as I’m staring in disbelief at the bottle on the ground. I’m on a bit of a sensory overload at this point and don’t know which problem to deal with first.

**[pbu03-0174]** — Author of “5 week old feeding behaviour completely fine during day but unmanageable at night”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Usually between 1.30-2am: baby wakes, cries for bottle, drinks 2 gulps & spits bottle out, we try burping, cuddling, nappy change, nothing satisfies her and she only settles for the 10 seconds she drinks her bottle then she spits the teat out and the cycle continues.

**[pbu03-0175]** — Author of “5 week old feeding behaviour completely fine during day but unmanageable at night”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Couple of things:  
> \- midwife doesn’t suspect colic or reflux or milk allergy  
> \- baby poos and wees normally and at good intervals  
> \- baby doesn’t poo overnight (told this was normal)  
> \- when feeding she’s held upright, burped properly etc etc.   
> \- low light conditions maintained, just bright enough to see poo and clean her properly  
> \- both husband and I are whispering/trying to avoid eye contact etc.   
> \- bedroom isn’t too hot or too cold
> 
> I’m at my wits end with her night time fussiness and I just am out of ideas!

**[pbu20-0061]** — Looking back: author reports feeding every thirty minutes for five weeks before tongue-tie treatment. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> He was taking in too much air (causing excess gas) and could not get enough milk in one feed, so I had to feed him every thirty minutes for the first five weeks of life until the tongue tie was cut.

**[pbu20-0075]** — Formula break fails to provide expected relief from breastfeeding. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> we tried to do one bottle of formula this last week because the 2-3 am feeding is killing me mentally but while he absolutely drinks it! he screams bloody murder when he's finished and will not calm down no matter what till again, husband and i deep sigh and i pull the boob out. its so frustrating! that last night i said "whatever its more exhausting that despite my nips getting a break, him screaming for an hour after a formula bottle at 3 am is more exhausting."

**[pbu20-0082]** — At eight weeks, author reports unsuccessful feeding and medication changes begun earlier. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> baby boy is 8weeks+3 days, started Neocate LCP on friday which he has with Infant Gaviscon (i’m going to ask to maybe be put on omeprazole instead but i find myself giving up on things before even giving them a chance because i just have no hope 😞 ) he keeps having very hard stool like solid balls on Infant Gaviscon, he’s been on it for 2 weeks now, he was previously on aptimel pepti 1 for 2 weeks but made him so much worse, he was fine for a week on it but then all his CMPA symptoms came back with a few new ones!

**[pbu20-0114]** — Five-minute feeds and falling asleep frustrate efforts to keep newborn awake. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

However, we’re currently feeding all day long with breaks between feeds ranging from minutes to an hour. Baby will latch, nurse for 5 minutes, fall asleep, wake up, repeat. I’ve tried keeping her awake with lights, skin to skin, swiping her skin with a wet wipe, etc. No luck.

**[pbu20-0146]** — Prenatal breast pain creates competing fears about expressing, early labour and future breastfeeding. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I'm scared because I'm only 34 weeks, if I start expressing there's a risk of inducing or accelerating labour. But I don't want to develop and infection and needing antibiotics.

<a id="pattern-p"></a>

### P. Support has to be usable: presence, leave, excitement and information do not necessarily produce care or the ability to rest.

**Belongs in:** §5 (partners and practical help); §7 (stock advice); §10 For 6; §§17.1 and 17.4 (format and help).

**Relation:** Confirms and extends; contradicts equating available time with help.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Format helps the partner who wants to learn; it does not create the wish`

**What should change or remain:** Separate willingness, practical work, emotional connection and the recipient’s ability to relinquish care. Three months of leave can include no childcare; another partner attends every appointment and saves money while admitting no connection yet. A parent can be unable to sleep while a capable partner has the baby. The parent wanting someone to hold the baby is a direct counter-example to the assumption that housework is always the useful help. Partners also report exhaustion, relapse, fear and lack of reciprocal rest. Retain supportive partners who comfort, fetch relief, attend appointments or help enough to change the author’s willingness to have another baby.

**Reading the evidence:** Keep incapacity, unwillingness and unsafe behaviour distinct. A partner with kidney failure is not described by the same mechanism as a partner who ignores care or a partner alleged to coerce or assault.

**Evidence (49 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

<a id="pattern-q"></a>

### Q. Family involvement can supply care or create fear, judgement, loss of privacy and demands to manage the relative’s feelings.

**Belongs in:** §3 (others’ words); §5 (relatives and visitors); §6 (disclosure); §10 For 7.

**Relation:** Confirms and extends beyond a visitor calendar.

**Current conclusion, verbatim:** `Family pressure concerns birth choice, vaccines, feeding, body size, risk, and who gets access during recovery.`

**What should change or remain:** Add family pressure around abortion and continued pregnancy, delayed or withheld congratulations after loss, explicit blame for loss, unwanted fetal-loss photographs, naming and surname decisions, intrusive sexual questions, food restrictions, threats about grandparent custody, and pregnancy disclosure taken from the parent by a boss or a snooping relative. A mother flown in can trigger old trauma while being the only available help. The boundary question can be whether the parent can safely disclose or obtain care, not just who arrives when. Preserve relative support as contrary evidence and do not infer every strained relationship is abusive.

**Reading the evidence:** Some announcements are withheld to protect against repeating a public loss; some are early to obtain support and meet no support. A request for privacy is not evidence that the parent wants no family connection.

**Evidence (60 quote records; distinctive passages first, followed by the rest in source order):**

**[pbu03-0052]** — Author of “Need to vent about my MIL”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> when my husband announced our pregnancy to her, she proceeded to tell me about her fifth pregnancy which resulted in a miscarriage due to neural tube deficiency. She shared in detail how she cried every year and still remembered the moment she held her deformed baby in her arms. Then asked me whether I took folic acid in the early weeks, then said “in any case it’s too late to start now!” All of this to me in private message.

**[pbu03-0053]** — Author of “Need to vent about my MIL”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> after the first trimester ultrasound, I shared images of the baby. She commented “he has a nice round head!” then sent me a photo of her fifth baby’s head. I did not read the message because I didn’t want to have the image in my head.

**[pbu03-0316]** — Author of “Dealing with a toxic mother in pregnancy”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> today after a few weeks whilst on video call I asked whether they had had any other thoughts re visiting - she said no, that she had “lost hope” to meet her grandchild when she is little, and i said I was willing to run the risk of the baby catching something so they could meet her 1 month after she’s born, she then said that  I didn’t seem to have any desire to have them round” and my dad said Xmas would be too cold for them to come, and my mum said “oh I don’t think she wants us for Xmas there” and I said yes I do, I invited you and I got really upset and started crying and said I didn’t want to speak to her anymore today. she didn’t check in with me or anything after to see if I (her heavily pregnant daughter) was okay.

**[pbu03-0317]** — Author of “Dealing with a toxic mother in pregnancy”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0178]** — Earlier family blame shapes current announcement decisions. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We unfortunately had a miscarriage last year and I have had an ectopic in the past. We announced too early last time an lost the pregnancy and his (not immediate) family behaved in an absolutely vile way. Talked shit about me, the miscarriage, said I told you so and we deserved it etc. to friends of ours when it was brought up. Just ridiculous shit. It caused a large rift in the family but somehow was still associate with some of them (not all) and there’s no way to cut them out completely.

**[pbu20-0237]** — Unexpected early induction makes visitor restrictions conflict with gratitude and family guilt. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> She bought nearly everything off of my registry, and the week she planned on spending with me was turned upside down when my liver started to fail and I had to be rushed in to get induced.  
> A month early. Doctor’s said to keep him away from visitors for a few weeks and I gave birth FRIDAY and I’m so torn between wanting to spend time with my mother and feeling guilty she paid so much money on the registry and to get up here only to barely be able to see me and not be able to see her first grandchild.

**[pbu37-0159]** — Author describes maternal claims to parenthood over the unborn baby and rejection of a grandmother title. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I know the true reason she wants me to move home is because she wants my baby. She wants to have control over my baby and wants to be more involved than us as her parents. The part that has made me extremely uncomfortable the most is that when she talks about my baby she calls my unborn daughter “her baby” she says she can’t wait for her to get here and call her “mama” I have voiced that that is not appropriate to me and that she is a grandma and will be called that. Her response is that “she is like a second parent to my daughter and she is too young to be called a grandma, she is the mama”

**[pbu37-0160]** — Pregnant author reports threatened grandparent custody claims. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> You know what my mom said? “Grand parents have rights. We are parents to the grandchildren too, I’m on his side. If you decided to keep my grandchild away I could take you to court and gain visitation rights and probably get more custody than you.”

**[pbu37-0256]** — An emergency at work exposes pregnancy to family without the author's chosen disclosure. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My boss called my sister and told her I was pregnant and hemorrhaging. She called my parents and told them. 
> 
> I had not told my family yet that I was pregnant. I am 30 years old with a finance who is my emergency contact and who was already aware of what was going on and meeting me at the ER. 
> 
> I feel my privacy had been extremely violated. I am not very close with my sister and again, I have an emergency contact and she is not it. So not only did he call someone who my work is NOT authorized to call but he told my entire family I was pregnant before I was ready to do so. 
> 
> I cannot get over it. I feel so hurt and I just dont know how to move forward from this. I keep seeing videos of people telling their families and how happy they are and I feel such envy that my opportunity was stolen from me by someone I'm not even close with. I dont know how to get over these feelings. 
> 
> Does anyone have any advice?

**[pbu37-0299]** — Early announcement intended to secure support instead receives withheld congratulations in case of miscarriage. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We decided to let family know earlier than usual this time, just incase anything happened to the pregnancy and we needed the support. He told her around 3 weeks ago now, and I believe her response was “I won’t congratulate you yet incase she miscarries again”, which I felt was callous, but accepted it, felt it kinda fair.   
> But as time goes on, and having not heard from her at all, no “how are you?” texts, nothing, I feel like it’s getting quite awkward.

**[pbu37-0324]** — Relatives' warnings about a large baby lead author to restrict food before resuming normal eating. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I was repeatedly told not to eat too much because I might make the baby “too big” and end up needing a C-section. I became so worried that I was restricting how much I ate. At one point, my baby was measuring about a week behind.
> 
> Thankfully, I stopped restricting my food and started eating properly again, and baby caught up.

**[pbu37-0325]** — Author lists family warnings that turn walking, transport and eating into predictions of harm. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> **“You don’t walk - you’ll have a difficult labor.”**  
> **“You don’t walk - you’ll get a C-section.”**  
> **“You get a C-section - it’s because you didn’t walk.”**  
> **“You don’t exercise - something might happen to the baby.”**  
> **“You ride a motorcycle - you might miscarry.”**  
> **“You eat too much - you’ll make the baby too big.”**
> 
> It feels like everything I do is somehow being framed as something that could harm my baby.

**[pbu03-0012]** — Author of “Extreme gender disappointment”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> For some context, I had a VERY abusive and volatile relationship with my mother, abortion was illegal in Northern Ireland in the 90s so she had to have me, and she pushed a lot of trauma about that onto me. She allowed my step father to SA me as a child and teen and even after finding out, never got rid of him, so I understand I have a lot of unresolved trauma around that.

**[pbu03-0013]** — Author of “Family seemed disappointed at my announcement”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My dad didn’t even say congratulations, just “can you afford it?” then complained at me for 20 minutes about some of our current baby name ideas.

**[pbu03-0014]** — Author of “Family seemed disappointed at my announcement”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I truly just think I at least expected a “congratulations, how are you feeling?” before any logistical complaints/concerns/reservations began emerging.

**[pbu03-0046]** — Author of “FIL icks me after birth”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

**[pbu03-0047]** — Author of “FIL icks me after birth”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> But my FIL insisted repeatedly to hand over my baby to him which was kind of irritating. When I refused a couple of times he was saying that I'm being over responsible suddenly and that really triggered me.

**[pbu03-0056]** — Author of “MIL hell 5 days post partum”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> “Why are you giving her a bottle and not breast milk? You know you have to drink something warm to make your milk come in, you should feel it”

**[pbu03-0057]** — Author of “MIL hell 5 days post partum”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My husband was actually tearing up from stress, and as soon as she left I started crying. She was legit making it seem like we’re abusing our child for waking her up to eat when the pediatrician said she needs to get back to her birth weight. Mind you she didn’t bring any food for us, didn’t help us with anything around the house and asked my husband to help her with her tax paperwork.

**[pbu03-0116]** — Author of “Third pregnancy shocked and worried but weirdly not upset. Needing kind but realistic words”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Now I am worried as I know my mom and dad will be so very mad at me (they already didn't approve of me having a second, even though he was fully planned). They will definitely tell me I am irresponsible and it will be an overall negative/worried reaction from my whole family.
> 
> My boyfriend's family isn't really in the picture so there is no big announcement to make there.
> 
> I am also scared about, at some point, having to communicate it to my work as I've only recently returned and I wasn't planning on having another pregnancy while working for them. My manager will probably hate me and I don't know how I will ever have the courage to tell them.

**[pbu03-0227]** — Author of “Is it bad I don’t want a baby shower because I’d have to deal with my family ?”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I have always felt like an outsider within my family even my extended family and I cannot deal with having to plan a baby shower and have any drama unfolding. I’m already feeling low everyday and find it hard to get through being pregnant with constant anxiety and pressure to be the perfect pregnant person. I also have a sibling who is a compulsive liar and I feel bad my baby is being born into this mess of dysfunctional family. Not to mention my in laws are also insane and no contact. I feel like I just want to be alone but also guilty if I don’t celebrate my baby. I have littt to no support.

**[pbu03-0382]** — Author of “AITA for calling out my friend for being weird about my daughters?”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> my friend said “yeah that makes sense that you would keep trying to give your husband a son” I said, “even if its another girl, we would be happy! We just don’t don’t feel like we’re ready to be done having babies yet” she came back with “I would never want a daughter. I’m so glad I was able to give my husband a son first try. I feel like any other kids we’re going to have are going to be sons too. It’s the most important thing to a man to pass down his legacy, ya know. I would just die if we only had daughters.“ ouch and honestly, gross.

**[pbu03-0397]** — Author of “I think I might be pregnant.”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Unluckily, my parents are incredibly unsupportive and I will be kicked out if I tell them. My boyfriend is non-contact with both his parents, and living with a close friend, so I will have nearly nowhere to go. 

**[pbu03-0434]** — Author of “Did you have your main first trimester systems early/consistently? Or did they change or come later?”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

Our kids will probably be the only grandkids across multiple branches of the family and I so want to tell people, but hate the idea of disappointing them if I miscarry or it's a blighted ovum. I'm also just so chatty, I'm dying to talk about it.

**[pbu03-0464]** — Author of “Friends’ reactions to pregnancy”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> When i first told them i was pregnant, one gave me a high five and then later told me “aw..1 in 4 end in miscarriages”. The other gave me a hug a d later told me about postpartum depression and that 1 in 10 women get postpartum depression.  

**[pbu03-0465]** — Author of “Friends’ reactions to pregnancy”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Their advice isn’t unfounded/silly. It is helpful but it’s also not necessarily fitting my current situation exactly as they believe it to be. But that also means my friend-support system is even smaller.

**[pbu20-0013]** — Pregnant author fears parental rejection if she returns to baby's father. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> we both are in recovery we both have jobs he has a place for us to live he wants me to move in already he wants to step up as a partner but I don’t know how to tell my parents we want to make it work again that I want to move out and go live with him . I’m just scared for them to be so angry they don’t want to speak to me again

**[pbu20-0019]** — Mother attributes previous miscarriages to exercise. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My mom is literally berating me about working out. Mind you I’ve been working out since my 20s and since I’ve gotten pregnant I’ve definitely toned it down. But she thinks lifting a 15 pound weight is too dangerous 🙄 She is convinced that working out has been the cause of my miscarriages.

**[pbu20-0020]** — Pregnancy precautions become a source of anticipated blame. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’ve been already overly cautious to ensure that I’m doing everything I can to maintain a healthy pregnancy. It’s been extremely stressful for her to constantly tell me every choice I make is terrible. I feel like she’s waiting for something to happen to put all the blame on me.

**[pbu20-0050]** — Four doctors already feel overwhelming; mother-in-law adds monitoring questions. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> She has been overwhelming me with doctor talks. Have you checked this, have you done this that. I’m so overwhelmed dealing with d doctors as is that her adding to this conversation is just unnecessary at this time.   
> I try to be polite and tell her I got it under control but she won’t budge.

**[pbu20-0055]** — Early pregnancy secrecy competes with wanting to share excitement. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

>

**[pbu20-0106]** — Mother uses relative's miscarriage to question author's diet and fitness. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0120]** — At six months, author recalls agreed vaccination requirement for newborn visitors. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0121]** — Author says visitor vaccination rule prevented her mother's support after birth. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0203]** — Relatives' jealousy feels incompatible with support through a difficult pregnancy. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Three, I had fertility issues and miscarriages before this.

**[pbu20-0207]** — Strained family relationship leaves newly pregnant author without maternal guidance. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

I don't really have any maternal figures that I can go to for comfort or advice either.

**[pbu20-0224]** — Author wants maternal support but expects judgment after previous miscarriage management. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> It’s hard because I want my mom to be a mom. And I don’t know what to do about it cause sometimes I just want my mom, but I know it’s gonna come with nonstop judgment and I don’t need that and I don’t know how to handle it. I’m sorry this was kind of a rant. I just feel so hurt.

**[pbu20-0256]** — Symptoms invite speculation before author is ready to announce. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My concern at this point is already having people comment/speculate that I’m pregnant because of the symptoms. I had to cancel plans with SIL yesterday, and she’s commented today to my husband that her first thought was pregnant. 
> 
> I’m still quite anxious and not wanting to tell anyone until after our dating scan, and just have no idea how to handle the comments/questions that come. SIL had struggled with conceiving before her first was born, and had a few miscarriages, so I assumed she would have the tact and thoughtfulness to not comment until we had said something. 
> 
> Really I’m just nervous that if someone says something before I’m ready I’ll say something to confirm and then if something does go wrong I have to walk it back and deal with it publicly

**[pbu20-0276]** — Cruel remark at workplace event intensifies distress. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

**[pbu20-0305]** — Author seeks permission to share pregnancy before twelve-week convention. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0344]** — Grief during pregnancy compounds disappointment with an unreliable friend. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0110]** — Mother recalls pressure to let the father choose the baby's name at birth. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

**[pbu37-0128]** — Mother describes being forgotten and denied requested support while bleeding after birth. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0129]** — A week of practical maternal help also brings criticism and undermining. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0153]** — Author is hurt that husband's first due-date response concerns his mother's schedule. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

I’m just so upset that he thought of his mom first because he’s already such a mamas boy. Should I be upset? **[pbu37-0182]** — Moving home for birth support brings unwanted delivery-room and month-long visitor assumptions. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu37-0185]** — Author fears others will resent her if an announced pregnancy ends. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

**[pbu37-0245]** — Friend challenges belly-touching boundaries and publicly asks about sex after exercise restrictions. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Service starts and finishes, she then comes up to me and says so are you really not gonna let anyone touch your belly? And i said yup. And she says not even me?! And I said yupppp. She just made a face and then asks a WILD question while we’re in church people praying literally 4 feet away from us. “Since your doctor isn’t letting you workout does that mean you can’t have sex with your husband like last time?” With a smile on her face keep in mind. I said ummm that’s not your business it’s private.

**[pbu37-0278]** — After many losses and difficult pregnancies, attempts to share excitement meet little interest. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

When I finally do I’m just on deaf ears. And I’m emotional and I’m just sad now.

**[pbu37-0279]** — During prolonged screening uncertainty, the only available family support reopens old distress. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> In a desperate need for help I called my mom to come stay with me. She isn’t the best company as she has given me so much trauma but I literally had no one else. But I am very anxious still. And stressed. My mom triggers my old wounds and expects me to regulate her emotions while I am itself a mess. I can’t ask her to leave because she flew from another country to be with me.

**[pbu37-0290]** — Mother discovers an undisclosed pregnancy through an ultrasound photo and pressures author to forgive the intrusion. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Second, back on my meeting and she comes in with an ultrasound that I apparently missed on the side of the fridge and says, “what’s this?” I was defeated and asked why she was snooping. She swears she wasn’t, said that isn’t why she came to my house. (We have lived here for three years and I can count on one hand the amount of times she has come over)
> 
> She profusely apologized, tried to blame it on one of my friends when they went out for the lunch. I’m still beyond upset that she took this from me. I feel like she could have clocked the ultrasound and kept her mouth shut until I was ready to tell.
> 
> Twice now she has told me I need to forgive her because I am being cold towards her. I have explained that this just happened and I’m allowed to take my time, her response: well it’s not like I stole your daughter’s social security number or something??

**[pbu37-0296]** — Author wants flexible periods of quiet in labour while mother-in-law focuses on bodily exposure. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Anyways, she is insisting upon being in the delivery room, which I don’t mind in it’s entirety but I keep repeating “I just don’t think I’ll want people in there the whole time, like I think I’ll probably want breaks, and alone time between me and my man. This is my first pregnancy so I have no idea how long it’ll be, and what I’ll be feeling or needing.”   
>   She keeps just saying “I’m not tryna see your who-ha” and honestly I’m less concerned with that than I am if I just want some peace and quiet for some time during the labor- that’s all.   
>   I don’t get why she can’t just get that. It’s really not that hard of a concept... we have had this same exact conversation at least five times just running in circles both repeating ourselves.

**[pbu37-0327]** — Comparison with another pregnant household member intensifies shame and unwanted thoughts about abortion. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> After my brother singled me out, I just went to my room and I broke down. I’m trying I swear I am, but I guess he doesn’t see that. I’ve been going to school as well and trying to continue my education through this pregnancy and have income that is helping out with bills and other expenses for the house. So it’s not like I’m completely useless. But he’s making me feel like I am. I just felt so embarrassed and not seen. He made me feel like my pregnancy and baby were an inconvenience. My thoughts kind of spiralled and I even went as far as to thinking about abortion. I’m even slowly starting to feel unwanted resentment towards his gf as I feel like she’s setting expectations that I feel like I just can’t meet right now even though I know it’s not her fault at all.
