> I'm 19 weeks pregnant, there was a small electrical fire in elevator room of the basement of our shared condo building (we live on 3rd floor). No units were impacted but the common areas of the building (floor hallways especially) smell awful, like smoke.

**Further supporting IDs, reproduced under other relevant patterns or audits:** [pbu20-0345] [pbu20-0346] [pbu20-0365] [pbu20-0283] [pbu20-0289] [pbu37-0246] [pbu37-0274] [pbu20-0100]

## Outliers and counter-examples

These are evidence in the same share. They qualify broad conclusions; they do not subtract from difficult accounts or supply estimates of how often anything happens.

### 1. Loss does not make every subsequent pregnancy more anxious, and a worrying start need not end badly.

These cut against an inevitable escalation of fear and a loss-only outcome narrative. They confirm the current findings’ insistence on a range, without cancelling the wait or predicting another record’s outcome.

**[pbu37-0131]** — Author celebrates an anatomy scan with no reported concerns after four losses. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> After 4 miscarriages, my husband and I just left our 20W anatomy scan. Baby girl was absolutely perfect and measuring a little ahead of due date. No concerns at all.

**[pbu37-0181]** — After missed miscarriage, author reports excitement and less anxiety despite acknowledging line darkness cannot establish viability. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I know darkness of lines can’t determine the health or viability but something just feels different this time. Last time, I couldn’t put my finger on it but something just felt off even from the first positive pregnancy test. But this one feels different in a positive way and I actually feel super excited and way less anxious this time!

**[pbu37-0213]** — Follow-up scan reveals a heartbeat after an inconclusive early scan and prolonged worry. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I still remember my first transvaginal ultrasound. Nothing much could be seen at that time, and I was told that it might simply be too early. Since then, every day felt so long. I prayed every night, worried about every symptom, and wondered if everything was okay with our little one.
> 
> Today, during my follow-up ultrasound, my heart was racing. I prayed silently before the scan started, asking God for strength and for good news. Then came the moment I had been waiting for.
> 
> There was our baby. 🤍
> 
> Not only could we finally see our little miracle, but we were also blessed to hear and see a strong heartbeat. The tears I had been holding back immediately fell. In that moment, all the waiting, worrying, and praying felt worth it.

**[pbu03-0294]** — Author of “Golf ball sized clots 7w pregnant”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> But then in the ultrasound, we saw and heard the baby’s heartbeat. The baby is perfectly fine and healthy despite my passing a GIANT clot.

**[pbu20-0367]** — Unquoted ending of duplicate crosspost reports a healthy birth after rapid reconception. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Thankfully, our rainbow baby came at 37 weeks on the dot. Healthy and the perfect, most happy baby who is now 15 months.

### 2. Intervention, long labour and feared complications can coexist with a strongly positive appraisal.

These directly qualify any rewrite that equates induction, an epidural, caesarean or a frightening procedure with a negative birth. Respectful consent and effective pain relief are described in practical detail.

**[pbu03-0072]** — Author of “Choosing a c-section”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> The section itself was quick, smooth and I felt next to nothing. Yes, the experience is a little strange and of course there are moments in your recovery that might be difficult. But there is no easy way to deliver a baby into this world. I think the recovery was actually easier than I expected. The first couple of days you can’t really do much but I was never really in lots of pain.

**[pbu03-0403]** — Author of “listen to your gut. i knew i had preeclampsia.”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> it was surprisingly very smooth! once i had some good pain management it went very smoothly and i pushed for literally only like 30 minutes ahaha and he just rocketed out! he's now in the NICU gaining some weight and learning to eat by mouth instead of a tube then he can come home. just had him passed out on my chest doing skin to skin and that was the best feeling ever😌

**[pbu20-0018]** — Positive second birth accompanies early bonding and calm after previous postpartum depression. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> And I think it’s affected my postpartum, in the best way. I feel so deeply bonded to my daughter, to both my children. Hormones have come and gone in their regular waves, my milk has come in, and we are adjusting to the nights of broken sleep and feeds and snuggles. It’s all been so easy to ride. I’ve had no anxiety, no depressive feelings, I feel like the best version of myself. I feel more human than I have in my entire life, and I love being a mother more than any role I’ve ever had.

**[pbu20-0159]** — After thirty hours of unmedicated labour, author chooses walking epidural. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I had wanted to attempt an unmedicated labor, and for the first 30 hours or so, I did just that! It was… intense! I’m sure the synthetic prostaglandins did me no favors. Counter pressure, a fitness ball and a warm shower are what got me through dilation from 1cm-8cm. I was making sounds akin to whale singing during the worst of my contractions lol 
> 
> But when it was time for us to go to the delivery room, facing some of the worst pain I’d ever felt in my life along with the possibility that my birth could end up in a C-section if I wasn’t progressing… I decided to get the walking epidural.
> 
> It was amazing.

**[pbu20-0160]** — Author judges epidural worthwhile despite needle-site discomfort. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Yes I do still feel a bit of discomfort in the entry point of the needle for the epidural. Honestly though, it was worth it. I couldn’t have taken the oxytocin without the support of the epidural.

**[pbu20-0220]** — Contrary account: feared complications occurred, but author describes trust in staff and acceptable outcome. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> During delivery, everything I was scared of happened- baby's heart rate went down, had episiotomy, internal tear, vacuum delivery, no epidural, hemorrhage, high BP and HR - however nothing mattered because you survive it and trust the medical staff that are there. Everything works out the most important thing for me was that my baby was ok.

**[pbu37-0228]** — Mother describes a long induction and pushing as worth it while admiring her newborn. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Had my baby girl in the afternoon via induction as I have GD. 
> 
> It’s 2am and I can’t help but stare at her, she’s literally perfect.
> 
> Had her via vaginal birth and it was a LONG process and a 2 1/2 hour push time but totally worth it.   Just coming here to say don’t be discouraged if you’re feeling discouraged during delivery.  It’s all worth it in the end!

**[pbu37-0249]** — Author praises repeated consent, comfort measures and rapid access to an epidural. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My nurse (an absolute angel!!) was so very helpful! I’ve never felt more cared for in my life. She asked before she did ANYTHING and usually nurses or anything were not fond of asking if they could do something. (Well that was before I switched to my current doctors..) Everytime she would have to touch my belly she would ask, she helped me take a nice bath/shower when my contractions worsened, and she helped me breathe through contractions until I couldn’t take it anymore. I planned to go without any medication other than Pitocin, and whatever else went along with that. But I also wanted to keep my options open. So after pushing through for a period of time, I asked for the epidural. She immediately hopped on to getting me an epidural. I have no regrets, that epidural had me chill out so so quickly, and I felt amazing lol, I would be a great sales person for the epidural I loved it so much😭

**[pbu37-0206]** — Author credits a small rural hospital with saving both lives. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am forever thankful for the care that my tiny, rural hospital was able to provide. They truly saved my baby’s life, as well as my own.

### 3. Later babies and recoveries can be easier, while a good first recovery does not guarantee the second.

The opposite-direction accounts should appear together in §9. These are comparisons within the author’s experience, not a ranking of pregnancy number or birth method.

**[pbu03-0161]** — Author of “PSA to all the first time moms who got delt a difficult baby.”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Yes! That was my experience also, my second one was much easier right out of the womb. The first one literally came out with his elbow and gave me deep laceration all the way to my anus, so you can also imagine my postpartum healing, but my second postpartum was waaaaay easier and almost pain free.

**[pbu37-0120]** — Third pregnancy brings perspective and a positive adjustment despite newborn chaos. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `partner`).

> We're in the new born trenches but I'm glad to be back because I know it doesn't last forever and time goes by so quick (even though it doesn't feel like that sometimes), baby boy has slotted into the chaos and the big sister has already stepped in her role.

**[pbu03-0147]** — Author of “Running on fumes”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I had an elective CS for the second time almost 2 weeks ago. The first recovery was a breeze but this time around it is legit the most painful physical experience I have had in my life. Every day I feel pain from a seemingly novel part of my body. My chest, upper, mid, and lower back, pelvis, incision site, groin, thighs, all hurt and it's been 11 days. I won't even get started on contraction pain - those have me doubled over screaming internally several times an hour. I'm dependent on NSAID suppositories to function atp. I have no appetite most times and I'm extremely depressed. My mental health was not the best to start with but now ​​I can't remember feeling true happiness anymore.

### 4. Support can be excellent, and severe distress can still occur.

These contradict a sufficient explanation based on absent partners or help ending. Keep the report of subsequent improvement, which belongs with the earlier suffering from the same account.

**[pbu03-0194]** — Author of “Sweet husband and thankful for this journey”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I had days where I would just cry my eyes out and my husband was there to hold me and encourage me. He would tell me how thankful he was that I quit smoking and how strong I was to be going through all the symptoms. He’d also tell me it was okay to not be strong. If I needed to call out of work he’d tell me to sleep as much as I could and he’d get me all kinds of things to try to help me feel better.

**[pbu03-0195]** — Author of “Sweet husband and thankful for this journey”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> During the 1st trimester I told him we were 1 and done but the way he has me feeling this pregnancy I’ll give him as many babies as he wants lol 

**[pbu03-0150]** — Author of “Scared to take Zoloft for PPD/PPA”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I had GAD before pregnancy but felt fine during pregnancy. I had so much support and no financial or relationship issues, I didn’t think id be the one to suffer from it. Well plot twist, my Dr diagnosed me with PPD/PPA and said to get on Zoloft.

**[pbu03-0172]** — Author of “Guilt and shame over post Partum rage at husband”. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m too ashamed to seek help from my health visitor or GP as I’m scared of what they’ll say and having this follow me for the rest of my life. The guilt I feel is immense. I have absolutely no rage against my baby she is so perfect. It’s just directed at my husband. He is also perfect and has done absolutely everything, especially the first two weeks when I was bed ridden and couldn’t do anything for myself except feed the baby.

**[pbu20-0123]** — Regret and diagnosed depression occur despite practical support and a year of leave. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> I have a 6 week old. She’s my second baby. My first is 4 years old now. I have a supportive husband, had my mom with us for 5 weeks helping with the new baby. We are financially stable. I’m 33 years old. I have a full year of maternity leave. My son is in daycare full time. We genuinely wanted a second baby and it also made sense to have a second with our situation. Now I’m regretting it so so so much. I can’t stop crying. The only time I can stop is when I sleep. I was diagnosed with ppd and I’ve been taking Zoloft for 2 weeks now. Although i started with a low dose of 25mg and am working my way up to 100.

**[pbu20-0124]** — Reply challenges expectation that experience and support would make second postpartum easier. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> Honestly I don’t care. I also know as a second time mom this will pass. I know it will. I had similar feelings with my first but it wasn’t this intense. It all passed and my son is the light of my life. I just don’t understand why I feel this way since it’s my second time and I have all the support possible and the meds. I thought this time would be easier but my doctor told me because I felt similar with my first and I had PPA, I was more at risk of developing mental health issues this time. I hate that I can’t get to enjoy the newborn phase yet once again.

**[pbu20-0125]** — October reply reports substantial improvement after medication took time. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> I’m feeling sooo much better! My meds took their time to work but then one day something just clicked and I started feeling 1000 times better. Please seek help if it lasts more than 2 weeks ❤️

**[pbu37-0269]** — Partner attends appointments, saves for longer leave and participates in preparation despite lacking excitement. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> He’s shown up to every Dr appointment, often times even asking more questions than I do during the appointments. He put a decent amount of money aside to ensure I could take a longer maternity leave if I wanted to care for baby boy. He actively participated in our baby shower (jack and Jill style), even paid for a photographer for us (that I found) and posted all of the photos to his Facebook page after we received all of them

**[pbu37-0270]** — Partner's fear of no connection coexists with preparing to support an imminent caesarean. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> He stated again how he’s not very excited, is scared because he feels no connection to the baby and “hopes” he will when he arrives. It makes me feel extremely sad to hear these things and while I appreciate his honesty around it, it kinda makes me feel like crap knowing he’s still feeling this way and we’ll be meeting our son in 48 hours. He’s all in board to be present with me during delivery and I’ve even caught him looking up videos of how to support me around all of it.

### 5. Someone else holding the baby can be exactly the help wanted; a willing handoff may still fail to create sleep.

This qualifies a universal helpers-versus-baby-holders rule and the assumption that scheduled shifts automatically provide rest.

**[pbu20-0076]** — Explicitly wants someone to take baby rather than cook or clean. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I keep seeing the popular take amongst moms going "I hate when people tell me to hand them the baby for a bit! no i want them to cook or clean for me" and wow i CANNOT be more opposite! I WANT someone non judgements to offer to take my baby for a few hours so i can take a nap and get some house chores and cooking done uninterrupted!!

**[pbu37-0067]** — After a difficult pregnancy, fear of partner mistakes prevents delegating newborn care. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I do this because I feel like if I don’t he will make a mistake. Ontop of this, when he is with the baby I can’t rest. I hate the way he picks her up, I hate the way he feeds her. I worry about him falling asleep with her (he hasn’t done this) I worry after a day of work he won’t have patience with her and something will go wrong. He isn’t as attentive as I am, or that’s how I see it.

**[pbu37-0068]** — An available sleep shift fails because anxiety drives monitoring and taking over. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> When I have my time to sleep, I literally can’t. I listen and worry. Often I’ll go downstairs and take over because I have so much anxiety. I am so burnt out and feel awful.

### 6. Clinicians sometimes listen, follow up, explain and make treatment work.

Retain these alongside failures in §5. The lead-exposure response describes escalation even when the team initially did not know what to do; the reflux account describes meaningful relief.

**[pbu20-0100]** — Reply reports maternity team escalating to emergency blood testing. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I called the maternity team, and they initially had no idea what to do, but they called me a few times with updates and escalated it, and I was rushed for an emergency blood test this afternoon!

**[pbu20-0209]** — After further dismissal, author returns to former obstetrician for assessment. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I tried that. I got the same run around and the PA refused to acknowledge that my chart says partial molar pregnancy. I became so enraged that I called up my old OB (I had switched because this place was closer. Big mistake obviously). They told me to come Monday and they will try to get me in for another ultrasound and the OB will see me.

**[pbu20-0161]** — Author reports reflux relief after OTC failures and prescribed pantoprazole. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> So I have had WICKEDDD acid reflux my entire pregnancy, that just seems to get worse throughout 😭. I’ve tried allll the OTC medications I could. Gaviscon liquid antacid was the only thing that somewhat helped, but sometimes it made me throw up if the reflux was too bad. 
> 
> I finally brought it up to my OB that it was becoming unbearable, and she recommended pantoprazole, a prescription in the proton pump inhibitor family. It’s pregnancy safe!!! 🙌🙌 of course, they want you to exhaust all other options before trying it, but I genuinely was at the end of my rope with the acid reflux. 
> 
> This stuff is a literal god send! First day I took it, I had almost NO acid reflux!! It’s been four days now, and it’s truly helped so much.

**[pbu20-0036]** — Reply reports recovery and switching to formula on doctor's recommendation. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> I'm starting to feel better and am finally getting to spend more time with my daughter. She's an angel and worth the pain. We did move to formula feeding at the recommendation of my doctor but she's been perfectly happy with it. I'm excited to put this behind me and be the mother I always wanted to be! Not one that has to lay in bed all day with pain and illness.

**[pbu03-0444]** — Narrative follows an apparent drafting-assistant preamble; assisted wording and reported outcome are unverified. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We eventually saw the head of MFM at another major Boston hospital. She considered the isolated UVV low risk and was comfortable with me continuing the pregnancy with weekly ultrasounds.  
> That second opinion, along with the calm and supportive care I received there, gave me confidence in our plan.

**[pbu37-0249]** — Author praises repeated consent, comfort measures and rapid access to an epidural. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My nurse (an absolute angel!!) was so very helpful! I’ve never felt more cared for in my life. She asked before she did ANYTHING and usually nurses or anything were not fond of asking if they could do something. (Well that was before I switched to my current doctors..) Everytime she would have to touch my belly she would ask, she helped me take a nice bath/shower when my contractions worsened, and she helped me breathe through contractions until I couldn’t take it anymore. I planned to go without any medication other than Pitocin, and whatever else went along with that. But I also wanted to keep my options open. So after pushing through for a period of time, I asked for the epidural. She immediately hopped on to getting me an epidural. I have no regrets, that epidural had me chill out so so quickly, and I felt amazing lol, I would be a great sales person for the epidural I loved it so much😭

### 7. Scary online material can prompt useful care as well as distress, and fear also occurs without previous loss.

These qualify both a stay-off-social-media prescription and an assumption that the anxiety cluster only concerns bereaved parents.

**[pbu03-0054]** — Author of “Positive pregnancy test after what I thought was my period??”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I looked for what felt like every little discomfort to be a symptom and had myself convinced that cramping that awoke me from a deep sleep was just my pelvis and uterus expanding at 6.5 weeks.

**[pbu03-0055]** — Author of “Positive pregnancy test after what I thought was my period??”. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> As the pain progressed I remembered this post and the comment thread attached.
> 
> I sook medical care and was diagnosed and we were able to treat it medically because it was caught so quickly.

**[pbu03-0370]** — Author of “I'm kind of freaking out”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I've never met anyone that has had this while pregnant. I didn't know the itching was an issue. I only called my doctor about it because I saw a tiktok skit where the OB got really nervous when her patient said her feet were itching. 

**[pbu37-0311]** — Unsolicited stillbirth images on Facebook distress an author close to delivery. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I'm 38 weeks pregnant, almost 39, and for the past week or two Facebook keeps showing me these fucking awful posts from people I don't know. 
> 
> The front page is now just groups and randoms that have nothing to do with me and I'm being inundated with posts supposedly featuring images of stillborn babies (?!!!!!) with people asking for things like image editing - "my stillborn baby's hands look big in this photo because of the angle, can someone edit this?" kind of shit. 
> 
> I report every single photo but Jesus Christ.

**[pbu20-0185]** — Pregnancy anxiety and repeated checking occur without reported prior loss. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I have seemingly no reason to be worried i’ll miscarry or something will go wrong, but it’s all i think about.. checking underwear for spotting/blood… is that a cramp? Is it normal to not feel so nauseous by this point? Am I doing something wrong. Im overweight, but in good health otherwise. Is anyone else in the same boat? excited but dreading your confirmation appointment? happy but worried to be too happy for fear of the worst? how is everyone coping?

**[pbu37-0289]** — Even an uncomplicated first pregnancy does not prevent fear of loss in the second. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am 5 weeks pregnant with my 2nd baby - I had no issues with my first pregnancy - but I am so terrified of losing this baby for some reason. We got pregnant on the first try and everything seems too good to be true. I’m trying not to stress but every cramp and ache I’m scared I’m going to have a miscarriage. Trying to find ways to ease my mind.

### 8. Unexpected pregnancy can be welcomed and prepared for confidently; sufficient finances do not force a wish to continue.

These counter a universal misery, unprepared-single-parent or financial-explanation narrative. They broaden §9 without making the people target buyers.

**[pbu20-0157]** — Single expectant mother has bought supplies, prepared room and attended classes alone. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I know I’m going to be a really good mum - I’ve already organised and bought almost everything we need, decorated their room, been to classes by myself, and done so much research on babies and how to be a good mum, even though I’m not in my third trimester yet.

**[pbu20-0348]** — Author describes joy after unexpected positive test and discovering thirteen-week pregnancy. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I told my boyfriend, he screamed out of joy. Anyways, I'm 13 weeks! Ftm' or anyone, Any tips? I am so glad, and so excited that I now have a human inside of me.

**[pbu37-0164]** — Author lists housing, purchases and practical preparation completed despite inconsistent paternal support. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Anyway, despite all this negativity and drama which has drained me (alongside pregnancy hormones!) I've managed to:
> 
> 	•	Buy a house, as a solo ftb;  
> 	•	Buy all new furniture for that house;   
> 	•	Decorate his nursery;  
> 	•	Buy all of his babies clothes;  
> 	•	Research and buy his pram / car seat;  
> 	•	Pack our hospital bags;  
> 	•	Sort out my maternity leave;  
> 	•	Ultimately keep growing this tiny human who I already love so much. 
> 
> I'm so proud of myself for managing to stay positive in a situation that I would have thought would break me. Though my family and friends have been amazing and so supportive throughout.

**[pbu37-0263]** — Author distinguishes emotional readiness and lifestyle timing from financial security, relationship stability and family help. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> The biggest reason we’re considering terminating isn’t financial or relationship-related. We both have stable jobs, live together in NYC, and know we want to spend our lives together. We also haven’t told either of our families yet, but we’re certain they would want us to keep the pregnancy and would be incredibly supportive. We know we’d have a strong village to help us if we became parents.
> 
> The difficult part is that neither of us feels ready to leave the stage of life we’re in. Right now, our lives revolve around friends, hobbies, and travel. We see friends several times a week, travel 5–6 times a year, and none of our close friends are planning to have kids anytime soon. We know becoming parents would change our priorities and lifestyle much sooner than we expected. At the same time, we’re confident that if we chose to keep the pregnancy, we would step up and be loving, responsible parents.

**[pbu37-0353]** — After infertility and loss, author remembers pregnancy-long happiness and the expectation that a baby would complete their lives. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> When I finally got pregnant, I was over the moon. Even though the pregnancy itself was difficult and physically rough, I spent the entire nine months buzzing with happiness. I was so excited. I thought having a baby would complete our lives.

### 9. Buying can be an act of hope, and refusing new-item prices can coexist with having enough money.

These qualify interpreting every delayed or second-hand purchase as an affordability problem. They are not willingness-to-pay evidence for a guide.

**[pbu37-0329]** — Author buys maternity shirts to make a pregnancy after loss feel celebratory. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Even against my better judgement I purchased some maternity shirts that I’m really hoping get use! It was a great deal I found; hoping this helps me feel celebratory.

**[pbu20-0274]** — Loss left baby supplies available; author prefers used despite adequate budget. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am currently 30 weeks with my second pregnancy.  Sadly my first pregnancy ended with a loss at 34 weeks.  Because of that I do have a lot of baby stuff, but there are some holes in what I need.  I don't care if the baby items we have are new and we aren't limited by budget.  I'm one of those people that if I can buy it used, I will because I think everything is heinously overpriced.

**[pbu03-0438]** — Author of “Really dark thoughts surrounding outcome of pregnancy, advice needed?”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I haven’t bought anything, have no intentions of buying anything, I’ve packed some of a hospital bag but only because I know, either way, I will need that.  
> I feel like if I buy clothes it will just be a) a waste of money and b) buying something that I’ll never use because of the above.

### 10. A plain explanation, a personal story and a playful format are not interchangeable preferences.

The app-style joke is one author’s form of sharing, not evidence of demand for a gamified app. The professional referral objection and the personal-experience requests should remain distinct from actual book/list requests.

**[pbu20-0212]** — Humorous software-patch format for pregnancy tracking; not clinical measurements. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> **v0.6.0 (Week 6)**
> 
> NEW:
> 
> \+ Heartbeat module now externally detectable
> 
> \+ Rapid cell division enabled
> 
> CHANGED:
> 
> \~ Resource consumption increased
> 
> KNOWN ISSUES:
> 
> \* Constant fatigue
> 
> \* Random nausea events
> 
> \* System stability varies by hour

**[pbu03-0037]** — Author of “Swollen thyroid (anyone?)”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Has anyone had swollen thyroid during pregnancy? If so, what was your story and how did it resolve?
> 
> I am 16 weeks and I have a swollen thyroid now. I am looking to hear other’s experiences with this.
> 
> Please no advice on getting checked or ask my Drs (I am already doing this)

**[pbu37-0116]** — Partner seeks guidance or a book for fear of losing identity and hating fatherhood. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `partner`).

> I’ve always been an anxious person and this has just ramped everything up to 11. I know everyone says things change because it’s your kid, but I am so afraid of the unknown/change that I’m terrified that I’m going to hate being a father. I’ll be the best father I can possible be, but I’m so scared that I have just ruined the rest of my life.
> 
> I’m hoping someone has gone through similar feelings and can guide me in the right direction, or can point me towards a book that might be able to help me get past this fear.

**[pbu03-0371]** — Author of “I'm kind of freaking out”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I just need some advice or encouragement. Maybe a streamlined list of MUST DO before he gets here (I'm a perfectionist and have trouble weaning my list down 🤦🏻‍♀️), comfort, personal stories, etc. 

**[pbu20-0166]** — Author explicitly asks for a book covering precautions, changes and warning signs. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Feels like it's such a stupid question, but how do you learn about being pregnant? What to do, what to avoid? Changes to expect? Warning signs? 
> 
> Is there a book or something I can buy? I'm 15 weeks and just going about my life and then I read stuff here like how you can't use retinol, eat deli meat and should never lay on your back. How are you even supposed to know this stuff before accidently stumbling on it? I feel like I wouldn't even be able to tell if something was wrong.

## What fits nowhere

The existing headings can loosely accommodate much of this material, but there is no developed subsection for the specific needs below. These are proposed additions to the synthesis, not reasons to remove evidence. The final item retains five attributed records that should not be made into a general parent pattern.

### 1. Loss and bereavement as their own stage and practical workflow

§2 has no developed branch for going home after stillbirth, handling shower gifts, changing a twin registry, or timing a birth decision around a funeral. Generic fear/recovery paragraphs can contain the words but do not organise these tasks.

**[pbu03-0242]** — Author of “pregnancy loss;”. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> i still can’t wrap my head around the fact that im not pregnant anymore because i don’t have my baby. that’s all i can ever think about is how i don’t have my baby.

**[pbu20-0213]** — Author wants concrete supplies and freezer-meal preparation while awaiting either miscarriage or hematoma diagnosis. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My doctor told me to monitor the symptoms and call if things get worse, but she can’t see me for an ultrasound until Thursday. I asked and her best guess is it’s either a subchorionic hematoma or a miscarriage. I’m hoping for an SCH and a viable baby, but want to be prepared either way. Anything I should bring to my appointment or plan to do today and tomorrow to make my life easier in the aftermath? Should I plan to make some freezer meals or buy any supplies at the pharmacy to help make either an SCH or miscarriage more manageable?

**[pbu20-0253]** — After stillbirth and maternal complications, author asks how to handle purchased registry gifts. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My question is what do I do with the baby items? I know most of my coworkers know what happened if not will find out when I’m back at the office. I kind of want to return the gift cards and not sure if I should do the same for the gifts a received. Idk if husband and I are gonna try again as we don’t have capacity to think that far out and we’re still grieving. Any  advice is appreciated it.

**[pbu20-0340]** — Registry changes and anticipated conversations make twin loss part of ongoing preparation. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I keep remembering every ultrasound and seeing him and wondering what they were both gonna look like, if he was gonna look more like me or his dad, when I sleep I dream of them both still, I think of all the twin things I have in my registry and how I have to change that, the people I’ll run into, the people who call me twins because I was having twins and the thought of having to hold it together when I tell them one of them is dead.

**[pbu37-0054]** — Author describes the embodied grief of bleeding and cramps after medication for miscarriage. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> I think the worst part is that, every time I flush the blood it feels like I’m flushing my baby. It’s weird, I don’t know how else to explain it. Every cramp that’s come along hurts more because these cramps aren’t to give birth they’re to take it away. I just want to fast forward to the part that I’m ok. That’s all, vent over.

**[pbu37-0304]** — Family funeral timing complicates a membrane sweep and hoped-for spontaneous labour before a planned caesarean. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> The last piece of this is that my **MILs service is scheduled for this upcoming Thursday, 9/17/26. My appointment and optional membrane sweep is tomorrow, 9/14/26.** From what I have read, if the Bishop score is favorable, there is approximately a 50/50 chance of going into labor within 48 hours of the membrane sweep. 
> 
> My husband and I are really hoping for labor, but I want to be able to be at the service to support our family. However, my next appointment would be 9/21 with a possible c-section date of 9/24, and I would rather not cut it that close if there’s a possibility of labor. 
> 
> What would you do? Any suggestions are appreciated.

### 2. Continuation, abortion, adoption and reproductive control

The existing birth-choice sections do not give these decisions their own treatment. Confidentiality, transport dependence, partner threats and reported procedure restrictions can alter the decision itself. This is research evidence; no service availability or law is independently established here.

**[pbu03-0129]** — Author of “Pregnancy Termination via nhs”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Im on dependant visa and my next to kin n emergency contact is my husband. Had very unplanned pregnancy as my mind and body is not able to accept it. Feels stuck and very disappointed with myself to get pregnant this early.  
> Anyhow now i want to terminate and i m around 6 weeks pregnant. What are my options to terminate and will my husband ever find it? Yes i want a baby but not now . Maybe after two three years.  

**[pbu03-0372]** — Author of “Argument with bf”. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I didn't want to be pregnant but my bf didn't put on protection at the time that I requested it and now I am "accidentally" pregnant. I didn't have access to emergency contraceptive or abortion care because I lost my license due to my illness and was dependent on him for transportation. I couldn't ask anyone else and he begged me to keep the baby.

**[pbu20-0205]** — Author feels trapped after partner threatens to leave if she terminates. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Some days he is SO wonderful and patient with me so it’s confusing. It’s just the frequency in which he socializes that frustrates me. Am i forcing this? Am i being irrational by asking him to slow down on the partying? Am i being too negative? He said if i get an abortion he will leave me and refuses to talk about our options. I’m starting to feel extremely trapped and lonely.

**[pbu20-0324]** — Ex threatens withdrawal from existing children if author continues new pregnancy. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> The dad is set on abortion and claims he wants nothing to do with all of us if I keep this baby, that's his threat. For me I will never be able to forgive the father for forcing the abortion but could get past it so at least the kids can have him in their lives..

**[pbu20-0313]** — Reported local abortion procedure and restrictions influence reconsideration and planned partner conversation. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Where I am they require an ultrasound prior to being able to make an abortion appt, the soonest appt was just past my 8 week mark. The only abortion option is a sharp d&c with no anesthesia option, Id have ibuprofen once in a recovery room, and im required to be at the hospital for 48 hours after the procedures with no visitors allowed. I'm not in the US but a remote location with only the one clinic option. I'm terrified of having it since i spoke with the doctor about the procedure and have my ultrasound coming up in less than 2 weeks. In this time i have thought about the pregnancy and the abortion every single day. Slowly I've wondered if itd be possible for having a conversation and discussing if he would be open to other options outside the sharp d&c. I would never force him to be a father but wanted to present it as an option. Saying "i love you so much but I dont think I can go through with the abortion. As much as i want to be with you I understand you never wanted children so I'm prepared to raise the child in my own so if you do not want this, it's okay" because essentially its my choice to have the baby but its also his choice to be a father. I can't take his choice away from him or force him to do something he doesnt want. But im nervous to have the conversation. Can I get some opinions? Am I going crazy from the horomones wanting to go against abortion when I was fine with it before talking to the doctor?

**[pbu37-0244]** — Author leaves after partner's assault but fears facing imminent birth alone. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> I am 35 weeks today with a child we both wanted very badly. We got into a fight today and my partner eventually lost her cool, tossed my food away, threw things all over the house (including my phone), grabbed my face and hair and pushed me to the ground, and slapped me across the face. All the while I was just begging her not to hurt me or the baby. 
> 
> I packed a bag and left right away. Now she is apologetic. I feel like I am a horrible mother if I return to her knowing what has happened. At the same time, I’m about to give birth to this baby in a few weeks and I don’t want to do this alone. I also still love her of course.

### 3. Disclosure and care that can feel unsafe

The outline has no dedicated account of fears about child services, hospitalisation, honest mental-health screens, controlled prescriptions or drug testing. These fears arise even when the author is sober, prescribed treatment or already in clinical care.

**[pbu20-0163]** — Fear of judgment and child-protection involvement obstructs disclosure of distress. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I want to talk to my OB but i’m nervous about getting looked down on since i’m already a young adult who is pregnant. I’ve also had this unshaken fear that if I brought up some thoughts i’ve been having that CPS would get called whenever the baby is here… I just really need some advice on what to do because I feel like i’m losing myself and everyone close to me.

**[pbu20-0271]** — Author conceals self-harm from psychiatrist because of fear of hospitalisation and responsibilities. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> Im so depressed, ive self harmed 2x this pregnancy and haven’t told anyone except my husband, i lied to my psychiatrist about it because i had too much to do and i didn’t want to be hospitalized again for it, ive had suicidal thought more than i can count and I just genuinely feel like a terrible mom knowing my baby can feel everything i feel. I don’t know what to do anymore, postpartum is going to be so so bad for me i can just tell and i have NO support system. I have no friends and both of my parents are dead, my husbands parents dont like me and his sisters can be iffy. I’m so lost. Has anyone else felt this way? What do I do?

**[pbu20-0289]** — Bagel article and AI-generated search information trigger fear about newborn drug testing. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> When I read this I figured I just wouldnt eat them close to birth but then I got to thinking and wondered if the poppy seeds could also set off meconium and umbillical chord test. Usually those arent routinely tested but I do have a perscription for Adderall which from what google said "its AI so gotta take it with a grain of salt" might flag in their system to automatically drug test my newborn since its a controlled substance. So my question here is, has anyone taken adderall throughout pregnancy and gotten flagged to have their newborn tested? And does anyone At. All. Know if everything bagels can cause a false positive on meconium and umbillical drug screen? If anyone at all has answers this would help reduce so much anxiety for me. I live in midlands SC which laws here are a little rough.

**[pbu20-0345]** — Prescribed addiction treatment evokes shame and fear of newborn monitoring and child services. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> It’s really frustrating bc I thought I was done with this chapter of my life and that I’d be able to have a pregnancy without this medication. I feel like a failure bc of it and like I’m a terrible mother for it. I know that my baby will have to be monitored for NAS and that it will be my fault for it, and even though it could be harder drugs like I used prior to getting sober I just can’t help but feel like a POS for it. I keep stressing myself out about how my baby will be when he gets here and if I’ve royally screwed him up, I worry about having childrens services involved even though i’m apart from this medicine that on paper I’ve done everything else correctly as far as my health, diet, doctors appointments, getting everything that baby and I will need, etc.

**[pbu37-0099]** — Author reports being pressured to change an honest prenatal self-harm screener answer. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> The first time I did it during my pregnancy, I answered the questions honestly. The last question asks if you think of hurting yourself and the choices are: often, sometimes, hardly ever, and never. I put "hardly ever" because that's true. Everyone basically bullied me into changing my answer to "never." The nurse literally said: "you put 'hardly ever,' but you meant 'never,' right?" Until I just got angry and said "yeah, if thats what it has to be." And then they just kept pressuring me to confirm i wouldnt hurt myself.

**[pbu37-0274]** — Author seeks a documented negative THC test to counter fear about an earlier positive result; policy understanding is unverified. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I’m thinking I want to ask my midwife if we can do another urine drug test so that I can have that negative on my chart. I just want it to be on record that I stopped using THC upon finding out I was pregnant and this test will show that. I worry about CPS involvement because of that one positive early on, but I know that in Ohio CPS only gets involved if me or the baby test positive for anything at birth which won’t be a problem in my case. My question is, am I doing too much? Is that weird to request a drug test? lol or is the negative drug test a good idea?

### 4. Sexual trauma, neurodivergence and bodily sensations

The current selector has no dedicated route for sexual trauma activated by feeding or immobility, autistic sensory/food needs, or panic at fetal movement itself. Their specific mechanisms disappear if filed only as generic anxiety.

**[pbu03-0015]** — Author of “pregnant with my second and looking to succeed in breastfeeding this time. (TW: mentions of sexual abuse)”. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I unfortunately found out when i first tried breast-feeding my son, that I have a lot of sexual trauma attached to my breasts, which made it super hard for me to breast-feed him. I felt uncomfortable doing it, and for some reason, I felt as if I was a predator if I tried to force him to feed on my boob. I know it's not rational, and I know it's not real, but unfortunately, sexual trauma affects you in the worst ways!

**[pbu03-0385]** — Author of “The hospital was kind of awful?!?! TW difficult birth”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> It really sucks because it was 100% effective for the pain. But the anxiety wasn’t worth it. Once I got into the OR and the surgery was underway, I wasn’t thinking about my legs anymore and was obviously thankful I couldn’t feel anything. And when I was in the recovery room and I started to feel my legs again, my anxiety melted away.

**[pbu03-0386]** — Author of “The hospital was kind of awful?!?! TW difficult birth”. (stage: `birth`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I do have sexual trauma and claustrophobia, and I can see how that might cause the reaction I had to it!!!!

**[pbu20-0246]** — Author explicitly wants experiences matching autism, ADHD, depression and limited support. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> This worries me, especially with a baby on the way. I don’t want to terminate the pregnancy, but I am scared about my capacity to handle what is coming.  
> I have very limited patience and currently very little internal space for another person, because even taking care of myself is already difficult.
> 
> Another important factor is that we don’t really have a support network. We will be doing most things on our own, with very little family help.
> 
> I can’t tell yet what is autism/ADHD, what is depression, what is situational stress, or what is just everything combined.
> 
> I guess I’m looking for people who recognize themselves in this: autistic and/or ADHD people who became parents while feeling overwhelmed or afraid of it. How did it go for you? What helped? What do you wish you had known beforehand? Please give me some hope

**[pbu37-0027]** — Selective eating complicates pregnancy nutrition despite access to food. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I 100% have access to any foods, so suggestions are welcome!! However I am autistic and struggle with selective eating, which makes it really hard to try new foods. I still try my best, I’m more focused on my baby getting the nutrients and stuff they need over my taste buds.

**[pbu37-0001]** — First-time mother panics at movement itself rather than interpreting it as danger. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `expecting_mother`).

> So unlike what seems to be every post on here, it’s not because I’m worried about her or what these movements might indicate. It’s just a matter of it scaring me as the idea of a living creature that is able to move around inside me can make me panic and those sensations are strong.

### 5. Pregnant dads, actual minors and histories that do not equal child count

§9 needs distinct history questions and inclusive speaker descriptions. These are observed voices, not a decision about whom to sell to. Keep the explicit correction of being called a girl.

**[pbu03-0189]** — Pregnant dad seeks exercise adaptations and equipment that remain useful after pregnancy. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Dad-to-be here, 5w. Have always been fit and have had a consistent exercise routine. Haven't lifted heavy since we started the TTC process, but now I'm trying to adjust my workout in a way that can be adapted for the later trimesters.

**[pbu03-0190]** — Pregnant dad seeks exercise adaptations and equipment that remain useful after pregnancy. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I like my current cardio and weight routine, and I'll admit I've never had the patience for yoga, but it's always recommended in my research. I'll have access to a swimming pool in the summer but anticipate that I'll want to be very solitary in my routine given the vulnerability of being out and about in the world as a pregnant dude.

**[pbu03-0275]** — Author of “PLEASE. 16 F. Help me.”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> However, as you’ve read, I’m only 16, in highschool.   
> I have straight A+’s, an academic warrior, taking honors and AP classes, and around the top of my class. I know I have a lot to live for, and I can have a baby when I’m older. 
> 
> But I am extremely sad thinking about getting rid of my baby. The thought of it aches me, knowing that he or she could grow into a wonderful person, and I would be an amazing mother - it’s just not the right time. 

**[pbu03-0503]** — Pregnant author identifies as a trans dad and describes support options for hospital birth. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> First of all, I'm not a girl, I' a trans dad.

**[pbu03-0504]** — Author of “Absolutely exhausted at only 5 weeks!”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> This is my first/second pregnancy so i dont know whats normal. I lost my first in June this year, as it was an ectopic pregnancy on my ovary.

**[pbu03-0505]** — Author of “Placenta Previa, Feeling Like a Failure (illogical)”. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I am exactly 31 weeks with my 4th pregnancy, but only my 2nd to make it into the 3rd trimester.

**[pbu37-0247]** — Sixteen-year-old asks about breast growth starting around eight weeks postpartum. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> So around 8 weeks after i gave birth i noticed my breasts getting bigger and they're still growing. They went from E-cup to a G-cup and i don't know if its because of some hormonal changes cause of the birth or cause i'm 16 and still growing as a woman or if its something serious.

### 6. Cancer, existing disability and children with specific care needs

Pregnancy plus serious concurrent illness and an older child who elopes need a capacity-based account beyond trimester and toddler age. A parent with arm injuries also fears being physically unable to hold a baby.

**[pbu37-0104]** — Author describes breast-cancer diagnosis and mastectomy during pregnancy. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> At the end of May I discovered I had early stage breast cancer at 14w pregnant. On 7/1, I had a total right mastectomy and I'm a little over two weeks post op. I was so worried about needing additional treatment given that the cancer was extensive and high grade, but it all stayed within the ducts. No additional treatment needed.

**[pbu37-0105]** — Pregnancy and surgical recovery collide with toddler care despite partner leave and practical help. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

> With that said, I'm so tired. I feel like I was so tired before all of this and now, I'm just physically and mentally exhausted. My husband took leave from work the first week and some change after my surgery and he works from home so he's able to pick him up for naps/meals since I cannot lift him.  With that said, I've had no time to just be. My poor son was so upset the first few days after being home from my surgery so I tried to be around and playing from the get go.
> 
> I'm in pain, on top of terrible back pain from pregnancy, and I'm trying to keep it together. I hardly sleep at night. Things still need to be done and one thing I realized is that...the world doesn't stop. It just keeps going. 
> 
> I should be thankful and so happy about this diagnosis. And I AM. But it just feels like, okay well that's that! Back to normal now!

### 7. Guardianship if parents die, and family planning across borders

A death-contingency plan and an older-child conversation after a thirteen-hour solo trip are different tasks from a hospital bag and labour-day sitter. They have no dedicated current section.

**[pbu20-0337]** — Pregnancy prompts practical questions about future guardianship when family is unavailable. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We have no family close by, I have a sister who has now moved overseas, and my husband has a brother. Don’t see either of them being willing or able to care for my children in way of that, nor would I want my children’s lives to be the way they live theirs. Basically asking, do your children have godparents, or do you have anything prepared if something were to happen?

**[pbu37-0124]** — Partner's denied leave forces solo international travel to discuss a new pregnancy with a child placed for adoption. (stage: `second_pregnancy`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> My eldests dad is also still my current partner. His Boss after promising he'd have the time off is now refusing to give him said time off. So not only will I be flying a total of 13 hours into 2 foreign countries solo, around 15 weeks pregnant with a medical lift limit of 20 pounds but I will also now have to keep my hormones in check entirely alone through this conversation with our eldest and idk what to do, how to prep, what to expect or how to not get overwhelmed as every time I even think about her being upset by this news I am gutted like slightly sobbing in the back at work.

### 8. Professional, promotional, third-party or unusually unclear material

The following five records are retained in full but are not pooled into a parent-pattern claim. The CBD research passage describes a study and makes scientific claims; the doula passage is a professional self-description spanning abortion, bereavement and crisis work; the sperm-DNA line is an unverified linked headline; the child-aggression scene is one father’s account of his own action while caring for a newborn and older child; the nonattendance scene has unusually unclear provenance/context according to the ledger. Their presence must not be treated as corroboration or clinical authority.

**[pbu03-0040]** — Student researcher advertises a pregnancy cannabis survey; these claims are recruitment wording, not parent testimony. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `professional`).

> CBD products are widely available without a prescription and heavily marketed as 'natural' and 'safe,' yet research shows that CBD, like THC, crosses the placenta and can accumulate in fetal tissue. Most studies have lumped CBD and THC use together or ignored CBD entirely. This study is designed specifically to understand CBD-only use as a distinct pattern

**[pbu03-0077]** — Doula lists areas of professional support while celebrating a caseload milestone. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `professional`).

> This week, I am celebrating my 100th Mom of 2026! Lactation, bereavement, postpartum care, birth, workshops, crisis therapy, baby care education, abortion, ethics consults, and a PhD candidacy…. just as I’m about to turn 30 next week! Feels symbolic. Tired, grateful, and appreciate the advice so many of ya’ll have given me, especially about how to better take care of myself when things weigh so heavily. Don’t let anyone diminish what a doula does. 

**[pbu37-0118]** — Father recalls pushing an older child while sleep-deprived, holding a newborn and supporting caesarean recovery. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `partner`).

> Two years ago when his sister was born, he was giving me a really hard time. He was hitting and punching me - while I'm holding a one month old - hitting me in the privates. I was telling him to stop, walking away, i was cornered in the basement, he's still hitting me, I'm on low sleep, my wife recovering from open surgery (c-section), and i grab him by the shirt, tell him to stop, and push him on the floor. I felt terrible about it.

## Money

**153 quote records indexed.** This includes stated prices, actual and proposed purchases, refusals to buy, free care, gifts/returns, insurance and care costs, debt, income, leave, housing and financial constraints. A stable-income statement is context, not a purchase. A price, willingness to consider buying, booking, completed payment and refund are different evidence. No purchase or valuation of the proposed Mamivio guide appears. The gift-return question below is not a reported refund. No explicit refund transaction was found in these quote fields.

**[pbu03-0348]** — Author of “I ordered myself an HCG and progesterone blood test”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> Me being the researcher and anxious person that I am, went straight to google. I found DrSays.com. It works like Teledoc or maybe like a Minute Clinic or Urgent Care. They have doctors that can write orders, it just won’t be YOUR doctor. DrSays offers a pregnancy package (HCG bloodwork test & progesterone test). Total was $46.97. You pay out of pocket without insurance and you make an appointment at a nearby LabCorp. They only work with LapCorp. LapCorp will not take your insurance card or any forms of payment because you already prepaid for the test. They emailed me the order, I’ll print it and take it with me to LabCorp.

**[pbu03-0358]** — Author of “First pregnancy”. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Confirmed pregnancy via urine test and was able to get an ultrasound done. Based on LMP I should be about 8 weeks. Turns out I’m actually about 5-6 weeks!

I could text his coworker (who I am close friends with) and ask her.

**[pbu20-0066]** — Difficult pregnancies, previous loss and practical capacity reshape intended family size. (stage: `trying`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> We've always wanted to have four living children, but between trauma from losing our first baby, having very hard pregnancies, very rambunctious kids, and finances/the state of the world in general, I think that we might stop at 3 living children. Obviously we will revisit the conversation later on since we are still ttc living child number 3, but I am someone who likes to have an idea of the plan and I get very anxious not knowing if this could be my last time growing our family.

**[pbu20-0074]** — A renovation during pregnancy triggers anxiety on arriving home with newborn and leads to moving. (stage: `looking_back`; speaker: `new_mother`).

> Once we brought our son home from the hospital, i started reading how popcorn ceilings (such as this one) commonly contained asbestos in the 50s-late 70s. Our house was built in the 50s, so it most likely did contain. Heavy, long term exposure increases risk for mesothelioma and other asbestos related issues. Risk comes from disturbance during renovation typically. These issues don't usually show up until decades after exposure. We learned that the contractor should have treated it as suspect and tested it. But that didn't happen. So this sent me on a 4 month long spiral (still dealing with it) worrying myself sick over what I potentially brought my son home to and what he might have been breathing. It was never confirmed to be asbestos or not because the entire ceiling was gone by the time I became concerned. We have since moved because of this situation. We were able to break our lease with no extra fees.

**[pbu20-0086]** — Unexpected formula costs add to pain, sleep loss and fear of discharge. (stage: `newborn_0_2_weeks`; speaker: `new_mother`).

I feel like I'm not doing enough even though I only get about 4 hours of sleep a day. We're supposed to be discharged later today and I'm dreading it.

**[pbu20-0110]** — Paid £43 for pampering while saving for maternity leave, then felt disappointed. (stage: `third_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> 
> The gentleman who did my hair was quite strange and I am a very very laid back accommodating person, I can handle anything and everything you throw at me and don’t get easily offended.  But I really can’t help thinking about how crap my experience was and how I’m desperately short of funds saving for mat leave and I had a really weird experience.

**[pbu20-0123]** — Regret and diagnosed depression occur despite practical support and a year of leave. (stage: `newborn_2_8_weeks`; speaker: `experienced_mother`).

Now I’m regretting it so so so much. I can’t stop crying. The only time I can stop is when I sleep. I was diagnosed with ppd and I’ve been taking Zoloft for 2 weeks now. Although i started with a low dose of 25mg and am working my way up to 100.

**[pbu20-0152]** — Between insurance policies, author hesitates to follow emergency-care advice after abdominal impact. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

Can you miscarry with no blood present? Can that kind of force kill the baby? I know the baby is well protected. Just would love to know if someone else had this happen and if they were okay.

**[pbu20-0165]** — Partner sees NIPT as unnecessary expense; author sees preparation for scarce rural services. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

My partner says they don't want us to pay for an expensive and unnecessary test just for my peace of mind, but I think that is unreasonable, because it's not about me, but about the baby's wellbeing when they are born. I know it's an expensive test, and times are difficult, but I think it's important to be prepared. Is it unreasonable to get the NIPT even at the absence of more markers?

**[pbu20-0166]** — Author explicitly asks for a book covering precautions, changes and warning signs. (stage: `second_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0181]** — Generic pregnancy-book descriptions do not match pain with endometriosis. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> But the cramping!   
> It’s not one sided or spooky in an ectopic sense, I’ve seen doctor already, but all our pregnancy books say ‚slight milder form of period cramping.‘
> 
> To me, this just feels like regular period misery. Nerve pain in legs, tummy cramping, nausea. Constant heat sensation. 
> 
> In endo, I read online, all that pain can just be because of the scars and adhesions in my abdomen and doesn’t really cause risk to pregnancy- but it’s still scary to feel. 
> 
> I’ve always known pregnancy would kind of suck, we’re aiming for one and done and will be delighted if we manage to pull it off once, and I’ll go through whatever my body throws at me just to hold my baby.
> 
> The idea of sex terrifies and completely disinterests me, gym has me nervous about any kind of rotational exercises (I have a membership to reformer pilates and that’s all twists and turns!) and the pain just feels so unsettling.
> 
> Any other endo women out there who can share insights? I’ve only ever read about difficulty to conceive, not really the unique pregnancy symptoms.

**[pbu20-0194]** — Author weighs attachment against partner refusal, work, childcare and absent family support. (stage: `unknown`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

> I (26y/o) have to terminate my unplanned pregnancy, even though I love that little pebble growing inside of me with all of my heart. But I'm too scared I'll be alone with my child, without the father present, because my boyfriend (30y/o) does not want to become a father at the moment (which is his right and I respect). Moreover, I know what happens to single mothers, how they struggle with finances, how it's almost impossible to work full time, because childcare is insufficently developed, how bad the mom guilt will be when oneself cannot replace the fatherly figure, the general stigmata sourrounding single moms. So, I feel like I have to prioritize what's already existing: my relatively stable life, including my relationship and workplace. To put these things at risk and gaining something beautiful that still will objectively bring so many struggles, it might not be worth it. Also, I wouldn't get any help from family, because they all live too far away.

**[pbu20-0200]** — Unexpected conception eight months postpartum creates painful financial and family-capacity decision. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).

**[pbu20-0273]** — Repeated branded home-test purchases conflict with negative clinic test. (stage: `first_trimester`; speaker: `parent_unspecified`).
